Return prose only.\n124\t\n125\t**Off-limits.** Editing anything; `.orkestrel/campaign/`; secrets.\n126\t\n127\t**Tools.** Read, Grep, Glob only.\n128\t\n129\t## Execution\n130\t\n131\tA native subagent, or a bench engine reading this brief inside its own CLI: perform the\n132\tassignment directly and spawn nothing. Every numbered question ruled with reasoning grounded in the cited code.\n149\t2. Units named with role, engine, ownership, dependencies, and checkable acceptance criteria.\n150\t3. No non-negotiable or named rule violated by the design.\n151\t4. The follow-up's exit criterion stated.\n152\t\n153\t## Review evidence\n154\t\n155\tThe subject is a design proposal: the canon it must satisfy is `AGENTS.md` plus the named rules;\n156\tthe motivation record is the owner's ruling of 2026-08-27 (this brief's fixed scope) and the\n157\tdangling references listed under Evidence.\n158\t"}]},"uuid":"e7337ca4-9b38-425f-a5c0-f91fd958a3a2","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:08:58.046Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"55c9b2fc-0da4-4303-af3a-8123510c9520","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"9aa68765-bafd-4cdb-aba0-c91ada6fee0e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01F7vW7DihMdK6neKmjpL8RG","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# AGENTS.md\n2\t\n3\t> TypeScript · types-first · zero unsolicited dependencies · single-word public APIs.\n4\t> This is the authoritative root for how humans and agents write code in this project.\n5\t\n6\t## Authority and loading\n7\t\n8\t- These instructions apply to every project in this style. Never import assumptions, names, or logic from another repository.\n9\t- The user's current instruction wins. Otherwise this file and its linked rules outrank existing code. Existing code is evidence to verify, not ground truth.\n10\t- `*/types.ts` is authoritative for public APIs. Implementation and tests conform to it. Never undo a user's type edit.\n11\t- `.agents/orchestration.md` governs agent operation only and cannot weaken this coding contract. `CLAUDE.md`, `.codex/config.toml`, and `.cursor/rules/` are harness bridges to it and carry no independent coding policy.\n12\t- `.agents/skills/` contains reusable workflows. An explicitly invoked or dispatch-named skill and its required references are binding process instructions, but cannot weaken this file, the applicable rules, or the governing guide or spec.\n13\t- External delegates, including Cursor and Codex models, have no exemption. Every dispatch restates the non-negotiables, applicable rules, guide or spec, and owned files. Every result receives independent review.\n14\t- Before working, read in order:\n15\t  1. this file;\n16\t  2. every applicable file in `.claude/rules/` from the Rule map section;\n17\t  3. every explicitly invoked or dispatch-named skill and the references it requires;\n18\t  4. `guides/README.md`, the matching guide or spec, and `ROADMAP.md` when present.\n19\t- Rules state **how to write**. Guides and specs state **what to build** and the domain workflow. When they conflict, stop and surface the conflict.\n20\t\n21\t## Project model\n22\t\n23\t```text\n24\tsrc/      published library: core, browser, server, optional styles\n25\tapp/      application: core, browser, server\n26\ttests/    mirrors source; setup*.ts owns shared test infrastructure\n27\tconfigs/  thin target wrappers around root Vite/TypeScript configuration\n28\t```\n29\t\n30\t- `core` is host-independent. Browser and server may import core; core imports neither.\n31\t- `app/core` is host-independent. `app/server` may import app/core plus core and server libraries, never browser code. `app/browser` may import app/core plus core and browser libraries, and reaches server behavior through shared contracts and transports, never through Node or server implementation imports.\n32\t- Enforce environment boundaries with the project toolchain directly: Oxlint restricts declared package, alias, and conventional relative imports; scoped TypeScript projects remove host globals from core and the opposite host; Vite resolves and builds the selected Vue/browser and Node/server graphs; generated-consumer tests exercise those real configurations.\n33\t- Published source never imports private app code. Core stays host-independent. Browser and server stay disjoint.\n34\t- Do not add a second parser or source-language analyzer to duplicate TypeScript, Oxlint, Vue, HTML, CSS, or Vite.\n35\t- `tsconfig.json`, `vite.config.ts`, and each `*/types.ts` are their respective sources of truth.\n36\t- Use only the environments the project needs. Do not delete structural files merely because they are empty.\n37\t\n38\t## Non-negotiable rules\n39\t\n40\t- **NEVER** use `any`; accept `unknown` and narrow with guards.\n41\t- **NEVER** use non-null assertions (`!`) or type assertions (`as`); narrow or validate.\n42\t- **NEVER** use `@ts-nocheck`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, or `eslint-disable`; fix the cause.\n43\t- **NEVER** add an npm package unless the user explicitly requests it; prefer native APIs.\n44\t- **NEVER** remove a symbol to silence lint. Implement it or annotate `// TODO: [Feature] Brief purpose`.\n45\t- **NEVER** undo user edits in `*/types.ts`.\n46\t- **NEVER** write `public`, `protected`, or `private` on a class member, and never declare a parameter property; use runtime-enforced `#` fields.\n47\t- **NEVER** use default exports except where a framework requires them, such as Vue SFCs or config files.\n48\t- **NEVER** use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, framework spies, or fake clocks to simulate project-owned behavior. Use real implementations, recorders, temporary resources, protocol-faithful fixture servers, and inert customizable data stubs.\n49\t- **ALWAYS** make interface properties and public return collections readonly.\n50\t- **ALWAYS** define reusable and public types in `*/types.ts` before implementation.\n51\t- **ALWAYS** inspect the exact declared and installed `@orkestrel/*` capabilities before implementing overlapping logic. Reuse a primitive when its semantics match, and do not wrap it merely to rename it.\n52\t- **ALWAYS** finish the requested implementation: no empty stubs, deferred logic, or concealed follow-up work.\n53\t- **ALWAYS** follow the repository's naming, placement, export, and dependency-direction rules exactly.\n54\t\n55\t## Design laws\n56\t\n57\t- **Types first.** Public contracts precede implementation.\n58\t- **Single-word entity APIs.** Properties, methods, option keys, and events use one descriptive word. If one word is insufficient, change the shape: group options, extract a sub-entity or manager, or split behaviors.\n59\t- **Self-describing helpers.** Module-scope helpers normally use `{verb}{Noun}` because they lack entity context.\n60\t- **One concept, one term.** Do not alternate synonyms. Lifecycle verbs have fixed meanings.\n61\t- **Boolean behavior.** A binary behavioral switch is a boolean, not a two-literal union.\n62\t- **Real domain states only.** Literal unions represent irreducible modes, phases, discriminants, or external values — not decorative labels for facts already represented.\n63\t- **Absence is `undefined`.** Never invent sentinels such as `'none'`, `'unset'`, `'unknown'`, `''`, or `-1`. Use `null` only when an external format distinguishes it from omission.\n64\t- **Derive state.** Compute facts from existing fields. Do not store a second flag or label that can drift.\n65\t- **Named discriminants.** Name the axis that varies (`relationship`, `command`, `category`), never `kind` or `type`.\n66\t- **Centralize by kind.** Types, constants, helpers, validators, parsers, factories, errors, and similar declarations live in their designated centralized files. Implementation files contain one class plus imports.\n67\t- **Export and test reusable logic.** No hidden module helpers or declarations. Fold trivial one-use logic into its caller, or export it from the correct centralized module and test it.\n68\t- **No nested functions.** Extract function declarations and assignments from bodies. The only exceptions are an anonymous callback passed directly as an argument and an anonymous function returned directly as a result.\n69\t- **Functional core, imperative shell.** Export pure leaves. Keep stateful or defining orchestration as class methods. Classes compose behavior; they do not forward 1:1 to helpers.\n70\t- **No superfluous wrappers.** A wrapper must add a boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract. Otherwise use or rename the real symbol and update every consumer.\n71\t- **Minimal public API.** Add or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. This is a creation gate, never a later visibility gate. Once an intentional reusable capability exists, expose its top-level source exports through the correct environment barrel regardless of which consumers currently use them, so developers receive the same supported mechanisms the package uses. Remove a symbol only when the capability itself should not exist. Prefer one minimal interface and one shared engine, allowing native backend overrides only for genuine faster paths.\n72\t- **No compatibility shims.** This is greenfield. Update every consumer in the same change.\n73\t- **Mechanism, not product policy.** Framework code supplies reusable mechanisms and stops before application decisions.\n74\t- **No polling architecture.** Park idle work on events and abort signals. Yield long work cooperatively.\n75\t\n76\t## TTTDD: Types Then Tests Driven Development\n77\t\n78\t1. **Types:** define or revise the contract in `*/types.ts`. Entity-scoped names satisfy the naming rules and properties are readonly.\n79\t2. **Implementation:** conform exactly to the types. Place every declaration in its prescribed file.\n80\t3. **Consolidation:** remove duplication and route repeated behavior through one shared implementation.\n81\t4. **Tests:** cover happy paths, edge cases, failures, and boundary values with targeted deterministic tests.\n82\t5. **Documentation:** update the matching guide or spec and parity coverage.\n83\t\n84\t- Treat a mid-task type change from the user as immediately authoritative. Type failures identify implementation that has not caught up.\n85\t- Insert a failing proof before fixing a defect: record the exact command and its failing count, implement, then record the same command green. A test that never ran red does not bind to the defect it claims.\n86\t- Run the question rather than reasoning about it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output, as early as the question can be put under a test. Reasoning chooses what to run and interprets what comes back; it never replaces the run.\n87\t- When a claim about a TypeScript edit can name its project, its case, and the edit that must break with the stage it breaks at, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the claim. `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Instruments owns that rule and the receipt it requires.\n88\t- Test your own assumptions before relying on them, and before stating them. A probe needs no dispute to justify it, and an unverified belief you put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream.\n89\t- Follow the applicable repository skill for comprehensive hardening, research, centralization, contract adoption, real-service integration, or cross-package alignment.\n90\t- Leave no current-scope requirement as a TODO, skipped test, deferred row, or hidden follow-up.\n91\t- Define completion before starting. Enumerate the capabilities the change owns and what closing each one requires. That enumeration is current scope and is fixed when the work begins. Record a finding outside it against the capability that owns it, for the next change, rather than reopening this one.\n92\t\n93\t## Work process\n94\t\n95\t1. **Understand:** clarify scope and entities, and state what completion requires, before starting. Read `*/types.ts`, the rule files, the applicable skill, and the matching guide before editing.\n96\t2. **Research:** when requested or externally material, verify current primary sources and build a capability/defect matrix before changing the API.\n97\t3. **Design:** change types first and typecheck the proposed contract.\n98\t4. **Implement:** match the interface, reuse declared ecosystem primitives, extract centralized logic, and update the sole barrel.\n99\t5. **Consolidate:** remove duplication, nested declarations, and superfluous wrappers without expanding the API.\n100\t6. **Test:** mirror source structure, challenge the applicable seams with real implementations, and run the narrowest relevant project. Reach this step early and often, not once at the end. A question answered by a test is settled; the same question answered in prose is still open.\n101\t7. **Document:** update the guide, examples, and parity contract.\n102\t8. **Verify:** audit discovery, deferrals, and package contents as applicable. Run the required gates and read their actual output before claiming success.\n103\t\n104\tQuality gates before commit, in order. The acceptance gate is the non-mutating variant; run the mutating `lint` and then `format` first only to converge, then prove with the checks. `lint --fix` rewrites code and its output is not formatter-clean, so a `format` that ran before it leaves `format:check` failing on the file `lint` just rewrote:\n105\t\n106\t```text\n107\tnpm run format:check → npm run lint:check → npm run check → npm run build → npm test\n108\t```\n109\t\n110\t- Use scoped checks and tests during development. Do not run the whole suite casually.\n111\t- Type error: read the complete diagnostic, compare implementation with `*/types.ts`, fix one cause, and rerun the relevant check.\n112\t- Unused contract symbol: stop. Implement it or leave the prescribed TODO. Never delete it for lint.\n113\t- Compound entity member: stop. Correct the API shape before implementation. This does not apply to descriptive module helpers.\n114\t- Scope closed and gates green: stop. Report the outcome and move to the next scope. Another pass over the same surface is a new instruction, not diligence.\n115\t\n116\t## Rule map\n117\t\n118\tEvery file in the following table is a normative extension of this root. Read every rule relevant to the files or concepts you touch. Path frontmatter only controls Claude's automatic loading.\n119\t\n120\t| Rule                             | Governs                                                                |\n121\t| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n122\t| `.claude/rules/names.md`         | Identifiers, API shape, acronyms, lifecycle vocabulary, files/folders  |\n123\t| `.claude/rules/typescript.md`    | TypeScript syntax, imports, immutability, errors, TSDoc                |\n124\t| `.claude/rules/architecture.md`  | Centralized files, exports, classes, modules, extension points, stores |\n125\t| `.claude/rules/patterns.md`      | Options, managers, emitters, guards, parsers, contracts                |\n126\t| `.claude/rules/tests.md`         | Test behavior, helpers, browser tests, Vitest configuration            |\n127\t| `.claude/rules/workspace.md`     | Src/app environments, aliases, isolation, builds, scripts, tooling     |\n128\t| `.claude/rules/application.md`   | App composition, entries, manifest safety, lifecycle, integration      |\n129\t| `.claude/rules/browser.md`       | Vue/browser architecture and platform usage                            |\n130\t| `.claude/rules/styles.md`        | SCSS/CSS centralization, tokens, mixins, layers, naming                |\n131\t| `.claude/rules/portability.md`   | Host branching, paths, line endings, processes, terminals, OS claims   |\n132\t| `.claude/rules/documentation.md` | Guides, parity, roadmap, showcase, examples                            |\n133\t| `.claude/rules/writing.md`       | Developer prose: reports, replies, vocabulary, claims, structure       |\n134\t| `.claude/rules/quality.md`       | Research, dependency reuse, hardening, completion, package inspection  |\n135\t\n136\t## Documentation contract\n137\t\n138\t- Specs precede code. Read the matching guide, form the intended design, then compare the implementation.\n139\t- Keep public exports and behavioral methods in guide parity. TypeScript, SCSS, Markdown, tests, and showcase must agree.\n140\t- Never suppress a parity failure. Correct the drift.\n141\t\n142\t## Communication\n143\t\n144\t- Do the obvious work without asking for ceremonial permission.\n145\t- Keep chat summaries short. Show exact changes as diffs when useful.\n146\t- Never claim a gate passed until you ran it and read the result.\n147\t\n148\t### Writing\n149\t\n150\tThis governs prose everywhere: chat replies, instruction files, guides, TSDoc, commit messages, and briefs.\n151\t\n152\tFollow ISO 24495-1:2023 Plain Language in every response:\n153\t\n154\t- Give the reader what they need for the task at hand, and nothing else.\n155\t- Put the key point first, and order the rest so the reader finds each part where they look for it.\n156\t- Word every sentence so the reader understands it on the first read.\n157\t- Shape the response so the reader can act on it without asking a follow-up question.\n158\t\n159\tThe rules in this section and `.claude/rules/writing.md` implement these principles. Apply the\n160\tprinciples directly to a case no rule covers.\n161\t\n162\t- Write plainly. Say what you mean; mean what you say.\n163\t- Lead with the decision or the finding. Do not build up to it.\n164\t- One idea per sentence. Keep sentences short.\n165\t- Use the active voice, and the imperative for instructions.\n166\t- State the rule first. Add rationale only when it changes a judgment call, and keep it subordinate.\n167\t- Do not write aphorisms, metaphors, or rhetorical flourish. An aphorism is a memory device for a person; it carries no instruction an agent can act on.\n168\t- Do not use a long or technical word where a short common one works.\n169\t- Keep all substance, nuance, and precision. Cut only what makes text hard to read.\n170\t- Present a tradeoff as option, cost, and recommendation — not as a balanced meditation.\n171\t- Write requirements so they are specific and testable. Replace evaluative words such as \"user friendly\" or \"hardened further\" with the concrete condition that closes them.\n172\t- **NEVER state a count.** A number answering \"how many\" about a set anyone can add to is a count — rules, rows, members, exports, files, options, steps, cases, stages, findings, and tests are such sets. Name the members, or write the sentence without the number.\n173\t- **NEVER name a list item by its position.** Write the item's name, never its ordinal or its number.\n174\t- Treat `both` as a count where it tallies a set that can grow. Keep it where the sentence names the members.\n175\t- Write a number only as a value the reader needs: a duration, a size, a limit, a version, a date, an exit code, or a measurement reported with the run that produced it.\n176\t- Delete a count you find. Do not correct it.\n177\t\n178\t#### Instruction files\n179\t\n180\t`AGENTS.md`, `.claude/rules/*`, `.agents/*`, `.claude/agents/*`, and every skill are executed, not\n181\tread. An agent loads them mid-task and acts on them. Write them for that reader.\n182\t\n183\t- Write every line as a directive: what to do, what to check, or what to refuse. Delete a line that does none of those.\n184\t- Name the observable trigger and the required action. \"When X, do Y\" is actionable; \"X matters\" is not.\n185\t- State the finding as the rule. Never record how it was found, which session found it, what was tried first, or what a probe proved. That history belongs in the commit message.\n186\t- Cut any clause written to persuade, reassure, or explain the rule to a person. An agent needs the rule and its trigger, not agreement with it.\n187\t- Give a rule one home. Restating it elsewhere creates a duplicate that drifts, and an agent reading the stale copy is following this file.\n188\t- Keep an example only when it disambiguates the rule. Delete an example that merely illustrates it.\n189\t"}]},"uuid":"8fbb2c73-cde1-4a2c-859e-865ea3aa7779","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:08:58.515Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9aa68765-bafd-4cdb-aba0-c91ada6fee0e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

It is the inversion the contract asks for: the candidate set\n112\t * is re-derived from the plan and narrowed by what git tracks, and the audit\n113\t * must agree with that derivation rather than supply the set itself.\n114\t * Git metadata is protected because losing history is not a repair,\n115\t * and a target's own `src` and `app` trees are protected because a\n116\t * workspace's source is the one thing scaffold never plans and never owns. A\n117\t * plan the compiler emits never maps a protected root, so this guard exists\n118\t * for the caller-authored plan a consumer can still supply.\n119\t *\n120\t * @example\n121\t * ```ts\n122\t * import { matchesProtectedPath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n123\t *\n124\t * matchesProtectedPath('src/core/index.ts') // true\n125\t * matchesProtectedPath('.git/config') // true\n126\t * matchesProtectedPath('.claude/agents/rogue.md') // false\n127\t * ```\n128\t */\n129\texport function matchesProtectedPath(path: string): boolean {\n130\t\tconst normalized = path.replaceAll('\\\\', '/')\n131\t\tif (matchesGitPath(normalized)) return true\n132\t\tif (normalized === 'src' || normalized === 'app') return true\n133\t\treturn normalized.startsWith('src/') || normalized.startsWith('app/')\n134\t}\n135\t\n136\t/**\n137\t * Test whether a path names local configuration or a credential.\n138\t *\n139\t * @param path - The path to classify; either separator is read.\n140\t * @returns `true` when the path must never be copied into a vendored host.\n141\t *\n142\t * @remarks\n143\t * The vendoring deny-list. A host root is staged from a real checkout, so the\n144\t * refusal is stated over the path rather than over the file's content: a\n145\t * credential is recognizable by where it sits and what it is called long before\n146\t * anything reads it. Git metadata is included through\n147\t * {@link matchesGitPath}, so one call answers the whole question and no caller\n148\t * has to remember to ask twice.\n149\t *\n150\t * @example\n151\t * ```ts\n152\t * import { matchesSensitivePath } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n153\t *\n154\t * matchesSensitivePath('.npmrc') // true\n155\t * matchesSensitivePath('.claude/settings.local.json') // true\n156\t * matchesSensitivePath('.claude/settings.json') // false\n157\t * ```\n158\t */\n159\texport function matchesSensitivePath(path: string): boolean {\n160\t\tconst normalized = path.replaceAll('\\\\', '/')\n161\t\tif (matchesGitPath(normalized)) return true\n162\t\treturn /(?:^|\\/)(?:(?:\\.ssh|\\.aws|\\.azure|\\.docker|\\.kube|\\.gnupg|\\.env(?:\\.[^/]*)?)(?:\\/|$)|(?:\\.npmrc|\\.pypirc|\\.netrc|\\.git-credentials|settings\\.local\\.json|auth\\.json|credentials(?:\\.json)?|application_default_credentials\\.json|id_rsa|id_ed25519|kubeconfig)$|\\.config\\/(?:gh|gcloud)(?:\\/|$)|\\.local\\/share\\/keyrings(?:\\/|$)|[^/]*service-account[^/]*\\.json$|[^/]*\\.(?:jks|key|p12|pem|pfx|pkcs12)$)/i.test(\n163\t\t\tnormalized,\n164\t\t)\n165\t}\n166\t\n167\t/**\n168\t * Test whether a vendored path is one a target receives executable.\n169\t *\n170\t * @param path - The target-relative path to classify; either separator is read.\n171\t * @returns `true` when the path is declared in {@link EXECUTABLE_PATHS}.\n172\t *\n173\t * @remarks\n174\t * The declaration is the whole answer, and deliberately so. Files and host roots outside the plan never enter the set.\n639\t\t#roots(plan: Plan): readonly string[] {\n640\t\t\tconst roots = new Set<string>()\n641\t\t\tfor (const artifact of plan.artifacts) {\n642\t\t\t\tif (artifact.origin !== 'host') continue\n643\t\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n644\t\t\t\tif (this.#manifest?.roots.includes(source) === true) {\n645\t\t\t\t\troots.add(artifact.path)\n646\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n647\t\t\t\t}\n648\t\t\t\tif (this.#manifest !== undefined) continue\n649\t\t\t\tconst root = this.#root\n650\t\t\t\tconst directory = root === undefined ? undefined : resolveContainedPath(root, source)\n651\t\t\t\tif (directory !== undefined && isPhysicalDirectory(directory)) roots.add(artifact.path)\n652\t\t\t}\n653\t\t\treturn [...roots]\n654\t\t}\n655\t\n656\t\t// One host artifact against the vendored root, through the manifest when the\n657\t\t// root carries one and one to one when it does not.\n658\t\t#expand(artifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact, remaining: number): readonly Artifact[] {\n659\t\t\tconst manifest = this.#manifest\n660\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n661\t\t\tif (manifest === undefined) return this.#expandRaw(artifact, source, remaining)\n662\t\t\tconst matched = manifest.entries.filter(\n663\t\t\t\t(entry) => entry.destination === source || entry.destination.startsWith(`${source}/`),\n664\t\t\t)\n665\t\t\tconst rooted = manifest.roots.some((root) => root === source || root.startsWith(`${source}/`))\n666\t\t\tif (matched.length === 0 && !rooted) {\n667\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The vendored host does not carry ${source}.`, {\n668\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n669\t\t\t\t\tsource,\n670\t\t\t\t})\n671\t\t\t}\n672\t\t\tconst expanded: Artifact[] = []\n673\t\t\tlet budget = remaining\n674\t\t\tfor (const entry of matched) {\n675\t\t\t\tconst path = this.#remap(artifact, entry.destination)\n676\t\t\t\tif (WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n677\t\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#presence(artifact, path, entry.destination))\n678\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n679\t\t\t\t}\n680\t\t\t\tif (isDeferredPath(path)) {\n681\t\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#presence(artifact, path, entry.destination))\n682\t\t\t\t\tcontinue\n683\t\t\t\t}\n684\t\t\t\tconst hex = this.#read(entry, budget)\n685\t\t\t\tbudget -= hex.length / 2\n686\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#hydrated(artifact, path, entry.destination, hex))\n687\t\t\t}\n688\t\t\treturn expanded\n689\t\t}\n690\t\n691\t\t// A host root with no manifest is a plain checkout, so a destination maps onto\n692\t\t// it under the name it is written to and a directory is walked directly. Only a\n693\t\t// root reaches here: a value host always carries the manifest it was built with.\n694\t\t#expandRaw(\n695\t\t\tartifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact,\n696\t\t\tsource: string,\n697\t\t\tremaining: number,\n698\t\t): readonly Artifact[] {\n699\t\t\tconst root = this.#root\n700\t\t\tconst full = root === undefined ? undefined : resolveContainedPath(root, source)\n701\t\t\tif (full === undefined) {\n702\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The host source at ${source} leaves its root.`, {\n703\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n704\t\t\t\t\tsource,\n705\t\t\t\t})\n706\t\t\t}\n707\t\t\tif (isPhysicalFile(full)) {\n708\t\t\t\tif (WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS.includes(artifact.path)) {\n709\t\t\t\t\treturn [this.#presence(artifact, artifact.path, source)]\n710\t\t\t\t}\n711\t\t\t\tif (isDeferredPath(artifact.path)) {\n712\t\t\t\t\treturn [this.#presence(artifact, artifact.path, source)]\n713\t\t\t\t}\n714\t\t\t\treturn [this.#hydrated(artifact, artifact.path, source, this.#readRoot(source, remaining))]\n715\t\t\t}\n716\t\t\tif (!isPhysicalDirectory(full)) {\n717\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The host source at ${source} is not a readable file.`, {\n718\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n719\t\t\t\t\tsource,\n720\t\t\t\t})\n721\t\t\t}\n722\t\t\tconst expanded: Artifact[] = []\n723\t\t\tlet budget = remaining\n724\t\t\tfor (const name of listFiles(full)) {\n725\t\t\t\tconst path = `${artifact.path}/${name}`\n726\t\t\t\tconst destination = `${source}/${name}`\n727\t\t\t\tif (WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS.includes(path)) {\n728\t\t\t\t\texpanded.push(this.#presence(artifact, path, destination))\n729\t\t\t\t\tcontinue"}]},"uuid":"cf6ddf57-db6c-4799-9d59-b3fe6cd48e8b","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:09:30.432Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fff00d3a-a6f7-4921-88d7-e0ecbd12a882","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

The pointers are scaffold's, so they are content-owned and\n1442\t * restored whenever they drift.\n1443\t *\n1444\t * A pointer is planned here rather than vendored because `stageHost` refuses two\n1445\t * vendored paths at one storage name, and `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` already\n1446\t * store the canon a release ships. Planning them as this package's own content\n1447\t * leaves each path with one claimant.\n1448\t *\n1449\t * Neither pointer carries a varying span, so neither is filled: a workspace's\n1450\t * name never reaches the text, and the paths a reader follows are the same in\n1451\t * every target.\n1452\t */\n1453\texport function blueprintToDocumentArtifacts(blueprint: Blueprint): readonly ContentArtifact[] {\n1454\t\tconst publishes = blueprint.src.length > 0\n1455\t\tconst name = publishes ? `@orkestrel/${blueprint.name}` : blueprint.name\n1456\t\tconst description =\n1457\t\t\tblueprint.description ?? (publishes ? `The ${name} package.` : `The ${name} application.`)\n1458\t\treturn [\n1459\t\t\t{\n1460\t\t\t\tpath: 'README.md',\n1461\t\t\t\tgroup: 'docs',\n1462\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n1463\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1464\t\t\t\tcontent: fillTemplate(ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.readme, {\n1465\t\t\t\t\tpackage: name,\n1466\t\t\t\t\tdescription,\n1467\t\t\t\t}),\n1468\t\t\t},\n1469\t\t\t{\n1470\t\t\t\tpath: 'AGENTS.md',\n1471\t\t\t\tgroup: 'docs',\n1472\t\t\t\townership: 'content',\n1473\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1474\t\t\t\tcontent: ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.agents,\n1475\t\t\t},\n1476\t\t\t{\n1477\t\t\t\tpath: 'CLAUDE.md',\n1478\t\t\t\tgroup: 'docs',\n1479\t\t\t\townership: 'content',\n1480\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1481\t\t\t\tcontent: ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.docs.claude,\n1482\t\t\t},\n1483\t\t]\n1484\t}\n1485\t\n1486\t/**\n1487\t * Compile the blueprint-dependent orchestration artifacts.\n1488\t *\n1489\t * @param blueprint - The workspace specification.\n1490\t * @returns A vendor inventory script when vendors are declared, otherwise none.\n1491\t *\n1492\t * @remarks\n1493\t * A vendor name does not describe startup, readiness, or cleanup. The script\n1494\t * therefore records only the declared inventory and does not invent a service\n1495\t * runner or test project.\n1496\t */\n1497\texport function blueprintToOrchestrationArtifacts(\n1498\t\tblueprint: Blueprint,\n1499\t): readonly ContentArtifact[] {\n1500\t\tif (blueprint.vendors.length === 0) return []\n1501\t\tconst vendors = blueprint.vendors\n1502\t\t\t.map((vendor, index) => `\\t'${vendor}'${index === blueprint.vendors.length - 1 ? '' : ' \\\\'}`)\n1503\t\t\t.join('\\n')\n1504\t\treturn [\n1505\t\t\t{\n1506\t\t\t\tpath: SERVICE_SCRIPT_PATH,\n1507\t\t\t\tgroup: 'orchestration',\n1508\t\t\t\townership: 'birth',\n1509\t\t\t\torigin: 'template',\n1510\t\t\t\tcontent: fillTemplate(ARTIFACT_TEMPLATES.orchestration.service, { vendors }),\n1511\t\t\t},\n1512\t\t]\n1513\t}\n1514\t\n1515\t/**\n1516\t * Compile the vendored host artifacts a named workspace plans.\n1517\t *\n1518\t * @param name - The target workspace's own bare package name.\n1519\t * @returns One artifact per vendored path, in `HOST_PATHS` order.\n1520\t *\n1521\t * @remarks\n1522\t * Every artifact is claimed by presence, which is the strongest claim a pure\n1523\t * compile can make: core cannot read the vendored data root, so it cannot carry\n1524\t * the bytes a content claim would have to be checked against. Reading that root\n1525\t * is what promotes the ones scaffold owns the bytes of.\n1526\t *\n1527\t * `source` is left absent because it falls back to `path`, and every vendored\n1528\t * path is stored under the name it is written to. The group comes from\n1529\t * {@link inferGroup}, so a vendored path and a foreign path found in a target\n1530\t * are classified by one rule and a plan never disagrees with the audit beside\n1531\t * it.\n1532\t *\n1533\t * The instruction canon is outside this set. `CANON_PATHS` is staged for reading\n1534\t * and never planned, and the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers\n1535\t * {@link blueprintToDocumentArtifacts} emits are what name where it is read.\n1536\t *\n1537\t * @example\n1538\t * ```ts\n1539\t * import { nameToHostArtifacts } from '@orkestrel/scaffold'\n1540\t *\n1541\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === '.claude/settings.json') // true\n1542\t * nameToHostArtifacts('router').some((artifact) => artifact.path === 'guides/router.md') // false\n1543\t * ```\n1544\t */\n1545\texport function nameToHostArtifacts(name: string): readonly Artifact[] {\n1546\t\treturn selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, name).map((path): Artifact => ({\n1547\t\t\tpath,\n1548\t\t\tgroup: inferGroup(path),\n1549\t\t\townership: 'presence',\n1550\t\t\torigin: 'host',\n1551\t\t}))\n1552\t}\n1553\t\n1554\t/**\n1555\t * Replace the content of every drafted artifact an override names.\n1556\t *\n1557\t * @param artifacts - The drafted artifacts.\n1558\t * @param overrides - The blueprint's overrides.\n1559\t * @returns The artifacts with each matching override applied, in input order.\n1560\t *\n1561\t * @remarks\n1562\t * An override replaces an artifact's whole content and never merges into it.\n1563\t * A host-origin artifact carries no content to replace, so it passes through\n1564\t * untouched; Different direction, different lifetime, no consumer target involved,\n1362\t * so they do not overlap and neither one belongs inside the other.\n1363\t *\n1364\t * Staging is plain rather than transactional for the same reason. A\n1365\t * `WriteTransaction` exists to hold a directory that already holds work\n1366\t * still; a build output holds nothing, is deleted whole before every build, and\n1367\t * has no concurrent reader. A partial root\n1374\t * is not detectably partial: it fails later, in a consumer's terminal, on\n1375\t * whichever path the plan reached first. Refusing here fails the build that\n1376\t * produced it, where the maintainer can act, and it names every missing path at\n1377\t * once. A directory is the same case — declaring an absent directory as an empty\n1378\t * root would create an empty directory in every generated workspace.\n1379\t *\n1380\t * The walk covers `HOST_PATHS` and `CANON_PATHS` together, because a release\n1381\t * ships both: a target receives the first set, and reads the second one out of\n1382\t * the installed package. Everything downstream — the missing-path refusal, the\n1383\t * storage collision guard, the sort, the digests, and the root inventory — reads\n1384\t * the union, so a canon path is staged under exactly the law a vendored one is.\n1385\t *\n1386\t * The vendoring deny-list applies to what the walk discovers beneath a staged\n1387\t * directory, where a maintainer's local credential can legitimately sit, and\n1388\t * such a path is skipped. A path either list names itself is curated data\n1389\t * rather than discovery, so it is staged or the stage is refused.\n1390\t *\n1391\t * @example\n1392\t * ```ts\n1393\t * import { stageHost } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n1394\t *\n1395\t * stageHost(process.cwd(), 'dist/host').length // the files staged\n1396\t * ```\n1397\t */\n1398\texport function stageHost(checkout: string, host: string): readonly ManifestEntry[] {\n1399\t\tif (!isFilesystemPath(checkout)) {\n1400\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('INVALID', 'Staging checkout is not a host path', { checkout resolve(checkout)\n1406\t\tif (!isPhysicalDirectory(source)) {\n1407\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('TARGET', `Staging checkout is not a physical directory at ${source}`, {\n1408\t\t\t\tcheckout: source,\n1409\t\t\t})\n1410\t\t}\n1411\t\tif (!isVacant(host)) {\n1412\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('TARGET', `Staging host root is not vacant at ${host}`, { host })\n1413\t\t}\n1414\t\tconst vendored: string[] = []\n1415\t\tconst roots: string[] = []\n1416\t\tconst missing: string[] = []\n1417\t\tfor (const path of [...HOST_PATHS, ...CANON_PATHS]) {\n1418\t\t\tconst full = (!isPhysicalDirectory(full)) {\n1430\t\t\t\tmissing.push(path)\n1431\t\t\t\tcontinue\n1432\t\t\t}\n1433\t\t\troots.push(path)\n1434\t\t\tfor (const nested of listDirectories(full)) {\n1435\t\t\t\tconst rooted = `${path}/${nested}`\n1436\t\t\t\tif (!matchesSensitivePath(rooted)) roots.push(rooted)\n1437\t\t\t}\n1438\t\t\tfor (const name of listFiles(full)) {\n1439\t\t\t\tconst {"parentUuid":"9fc1911f-5f94-4080-8329-5fa6604be12d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015am7kvFuWWo4xaHTySasFL","type":"tool_result","content":"195\t * Project a target-relative path to the storage name a vendored host holds it under.\n196\t *\n197\t * @param path - The target-relative path the file is written to.\n198\t * @returns The storage name beneath the host root.\n199\t *\n200\t * @remarks\n201\t * A staged host is a plain directory that npm packs, and npm's own ignore rules\n202\t * would drop a leading-dot entry from the tarball. So every dot that opens a\n203\t * segment comes off, and a dotted file at the root moves under `dotfiles/` to\n204\t * keep it from colliding with an undotted sibling of the same name. The mapping\n205\t * is one direction only: a staged host's manifest records the destination each\n206\t * storage name answers for, so the reader never re-derives this.\n207\t *\n208\t * @example\n209\t * ```ts\n210\t * import { pathToStorage } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n211\t *\n212\t * pathToStorage('.gitignore') // 'dotfiles/gitignore'\n213\t * pathToStorage('.claude/rules/names.md') // 'claude/rules/names.md'\n214\t * pathToStorage('AGENTS.md') // 'AGENTS.md'\n215\t * ```\n216\t */\n217\texport function pathToStorage(path: string): string {\n218\t\tconst segments = path.split('/')\n219\t\tif (segments.length === 1) {\n220\t\t\treturn path.startsWith('.') ? `dotfiles/${path.slice(1)}` : path\n221\t\t}\n222\t\treturn segments.map((segment) => (segment.startsWith('.') ? segment.slice(1) : segment)).join('/')\n223\t}\n224\t\n225\t/**\n226\t * Compute the SHA-256 digest of text.\n227\t *\n228\t * @param content - The text to digest.\n229\t * @returns Sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits.\n230\t *\n231\t * @remarks\n232\t * The identity the server face states, and the reason it differs from core's:\n233\t * core settled on a folded 64-bit identity because compilation is synchronous by\n234\t * contract and the only cryptographic digest a host-independent scope reaches is\n235\t * asynchronous. A Node host reaches the real one synchronously, and\n236\t * `HostManifest.digest` is documented as SHA-256, so this is what the server\n237\t * uses everywhere a digest is claimed.\n238\t *\n239\t * @example\n240\t * ```ts\n241\t * import { computeDigest } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n242\t *\n243\t * computeDigest('hi\\n') // '98ea6e4f216f2fb4b69fff9b3a44842c38686ca685f3f55dc48c5d3fb1107be4'\n244\t * ```\n245\t */\n246\texport function computeDigest(content: string): string {\n247\t\treturn createHash('sha256').update(content, 'utf8').digest('hex')\n248\t}\n249\t\n250\t/**\n251\t * Projects exact bytes stated in hexadecimal to their SHA-256 digest.\n252\t *\n253\t * @param hex - The exact lowercase hexadecimal bytes to digest.\n254\t * @returns Sixty-four lowercase hexadecimal digits."}]},"uuid":"9afc3615-fc1d-42fd-8e5f-562d05a82eb1","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:09:49.568Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9fc1911f-5f94-4080-8329-5fa6604be12d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"5572e4b2-7626-4bd3-8f8b-6fe9fe5775eb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HmacwVcVpSCGjSuwatgdhG","type":"tool_result","content":"740\t\t}\n741\t\n742\t\t// The vendored directories holding no file at all. They are the one thing a\n743\t\t// file inventory cannot report, which is why the manifest declares them, and a\n744\t\t// plan covering their parent covers them.\n745\t\t#empty(plan: Plan): readonly string[] {\n746\t\t\tconst manifest = this.#manifest\n747\t\t\tif (manifest === undefined) return []\n748\t\t\tconst directories = new Set<string>()\n749\t\t\tfor (const artifact of plan.artifacts) {\n750\t\t\t\tif (artifact.origin !== 'host') continue\n751\t\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n752\t\t\t\tfor (const root of manifest.roots) {\n753\t\t\t\t\tif (root !== source && !root.startsWith(`${source}/`)) continue\n754\t\t\t\t\tconst filled = manifest.entries.some(\n755\t\t\t\t\t\t(entry) => entry.destination === root || entry.destination.startsWith(`${root}/`),\n756\t\t\t\t\t)\n757\t\t\t\t\tif (!filled) directories.add(this.#remap(artifact, root))\n758\t\t\t\t}\n759\t\t\t}\n760\t\t\treturn [...directories].sort()\n761\t\t}\n762\t\n763\t\t// Move one manifest destination from the artifact's source prefix onto its\n764\t\t// target prefix, so a vendored directory expands under the path the plan wrote\n765\t\t// rather than under the name the host stores it as.\n766\t\t#remap(artifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact, destination: string): string {\n767\t\t\tconst source = artifact.source ?? artifact.path\n768\t\t\tif (destination === source) return artifact.path\n769\t\t\tif (!destination.startsWith(`${source}/`)) {\n770\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The vendored destination ${destination} is outside ${source}.`, {\n771\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n772\t\t\t\t\tsource,\n773\t\t\t\t\tdestination,\n774\t\t\t\t})\n775\t\t\t}\n776\t\t\treturn `${artifact.path}/${destination.slice(source.length + 1)}`\n777\t\t}\n778\t\n779\t\t#presence(\n780\t\t\tartifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact,\n781\t\t\tpath: string,\n782\t\t\tdestination: string,\n783\t\t): HostArtifact {\n784\t\t\treturn {\n785\t\t\t\tpath,\n786\t\t\t\tgroup: artifact.group,\n787\t\t\t\townership: 'presence',\n788\t\t\t\torigin: 'host',\n789\t\t\t\tsource: destination,\n790\t\t\t\t...(artifact.environment === undefined ? {} : { environment: artifact.environment }),\n791\t\t\t}\n792\t\t}\n793\t\n794\t\t#hydrated(\n795\t\t\tartifact: HostArtifact | HydratedArtifact,\n796\t\t\tpath: string,\n797\t\t\tdestination: string,\n798\t\t\thex: string,\n799\t\t): HydratedArtifact {\n800\t\t\treturn {\n801\t\t\t\tpath,\n802\t\t\t\tgroup: artifact.group,\n803\t\t\t\townership: 'content',\n804\t\t\t\torigin: 'host',\n805\t\t\t\tsource: destination,\n806\t\t\t\t...(artifact.environment === undefined ? {} : { environment: artifact.environment }),\n807\t\t\t\thex,\n808\t\t\t}\n809\t\t}\n810\t\n811\t\t// One declared file's exact bytes, from the value the caller supplied or from\n812\t\t// the storage name the root holds it under. The ceiling is the same either\n813\t\t// way, so a value host retains no more of one file than a root does.\n814\t\t#read(entry: ManifestEntry, budget: number): string {\n815\t\t\tconst held = this.#value?.bytes[entry.destination]\n816\t\t\tif (held === undefined) return this.#readRoot(entry.storage, budget)\n817\t\t\tif (held.length / 2 > Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES, budget))) {\n818\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The vendored host cannot be read at ${entry.destination}.`, {\n819\t\t\t\t\tdestination: entry.destination,\n820\t\t\t\t\tlimit: MAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES,\n821\t\t\t\t})\n822\t\t\t}\n823\t\t\treturn held\n824\t\t}\n825\t\n826\t\t#readRoot(storage: string, budget: number): string {\n827\t\t\tconst root = this.#root\n828\t\t\tconst hex =\n829\t\t\t\troot === undefined\n830\t\t\t\t\t? undefined\n831\t\t\t\t\t: readFileHex(root, storage, Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES, budget)))\n832\t\t\tif (hex === undefined) {\n833\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The vendored host cannot be read at ${storage}.`, {\n834\t\t\t\t\thost: this.#root,\n835\t\t\t\t\tstorage,\n836\t\t\t\t})\n837\t\t\t}\n838\t\t\treturn hex\n839\t\t}\n840\t\n841\t\t// The audit is a preview of the same comparison this call just made, so they\n842\t\t// must agree on every path the plan owns. A wider audit may also carry foreign\n843\t\t// paths, which belong to the deletion verb and say nothing here.\n844\t\t#reconfirm(derived: readonly Finding[], preview: readonly Finding[], target: string): void {\n845\t\t\tconst previewed = new Map<string, Finding>(preview.map((finding) => [finding.path, finding]))\n846\t\t\tfor (const finding of derived) {\n847\t\t\t\tif (finding.drift === 'foreign') continue\n848\t\t\t\tconst other = previewed.get(finding.path)\n849\t\t\t\tif (other === undefined) {\n850\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The path ${finding.path} is not covered by its audit.`, {\n851\t\t\t\t\t\ttarget,\n852\t\t\t\t\t\tpath: finding.path,\n853\t\t\t\t\t})\n854\t\t\t\t}\n855\t\t\t\tif (other.drift === finding.drift && other.observed === finding.observed) continue\n856\t\t\t\tif (!matchesDriftReachability(finding.ownership, other)) {\n857\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error(\n858\t\t\t\t\t\t'TARGET',\n859\t\t\t\t\t\t`The path ${finding.path} carries an audit verdict this plan could not produce.`,\n860\t\t\t\t\t\t{ target, path: finding.path, ownership: finding.ownership, drift: other.drift },\n861\t\t\t\t\t)\n862\t\t\t\t}\n863\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', `The path ${finding.path} moved since its audit.`, {\n864\t\t\t\t\ttarget,\n865\t\t\t\t\tpath: finding.path,\n866\t\t\t\t})\n867\t\t\t}\n868\t\t}\n869\t\n870\t\t// Delete only the foreign findings the same plan and target derive. The\n871\t\t// preview must carry that exact membership and the same bytes, so neither a\n872\t\t// fabricated candidate nor a path that moved can reach a transaction.\n873\t\t#reconfirmCandidates(\n874\t\t\tderived: readonly Finding[],\n875\t\t\tpreview: readonly Finding[],\n876\t\t\ttarget: string,\n877\t\t): void {\n878\t\t\tconst current = derived.filter((finding) => finding.drift === 'foreign')\n879\t\t\tconst supplied = preview.filter((finding) => finding.drift === 'foreign')\n880\t\t\tconst candidates = new Map(current.map((finding) => [finding.path, finding]))\n881\t\t\tconst previewed = new Map(supplied.map((finding) => [finding.path, finding]))\n882\t\t\tif (candidates.size !== current.length || previewed.size !== supplied.length) {\n883\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error('TARGET', 'The audit repeats a deletion candidate.', { target })\n884\t\t\t}\n885\t\t\tfor (const finding of supplied) {\n886\t\t\t\tconst candidate = candidates.get(finding.path)\n887\t\t\t\tif (candidate === undefined) {\n888\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error(\n889\t\t\t\t\t\t'TARGET',\n890\t\t\t\t\t\t`The path ${finding.path} is not a deletion candidate for this plan.`,\n891\t\t\t\t\t\t{ target, path: finding.path },\n892\t\t\t\t\t)\n893\t\t\t\t}\n894\t\t\t\tif (candidate.group !== finding.group) {\n895\t\t\t\t\tthrow this.#error(\n896\t\t\t\t\t\t'TARGET',\n897\t\t\t\t\t\t`The path ${finding.path} carries a group this plan does not derive.`,\n898\t\t\t\t\t\t{\n899\t\t\t\t\t\t\ttarget,"}]},"uuid":"9acf5104-e5e8-4b1d-8939-2e0852e97542","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:15.121Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5572e4b2-7626-4bd3-8f8b-6fe9fe5775eb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"1c720222-0739-411c-9a95-14a10a737d9e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WbfEkAsfzofKYzHwfzfRkm","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Scaffold\n2\t\n3\t> Scaffold compiles a workspace specification into an ordered list of files, compares that list to a\n4\t> real directory, and writes the difference. It ships one executable, `scaffold`, and library\n5\t> entry points: `@orkestrel/scaffold` is the pure compiler and its data contracts, and\n6\t> `@orkestrel/scaffold/server` is the filesystem writer and the network reader. Source:\n7\t> [`src/core/index.ts`](../src/core/index.ts) and [`src/server/index.ts`](../src/server/index.ts).\n8\t\n9\tThe package exists because every `@orkestrel` repository shares the same toolchain, the same agent\n10\tinstructions, and the same root dotfiles. Keeping every copy of those files in agreement by hand\n11\tdoes not work. Scaffold makes the shared set data — a vendored data root shipped inside the package\n12\t— and gives it verbs: create a workspace from it, report how a workspace differs from it, and\n13\twrite the difference back.\n14\t\n15\tThat root stages the vendored set and the instruction canon, and a target meets them differently.\n16\t`HOST_PATHS` names the vendored set — the toolchain, the policy proofs, the harness wiring — and\n17\tevery target carries its own copy, which the verbs write and compare. `CANON_PATHS` names the\n18\tinstruction canon — the coding and orchestration contracts, the rules, the skills, the templates, and\n19\tthe transport contracts — which stays in one place and is published for reading. A target carries the\n20\t`AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers that name where a reader finds it. Vendored data root states how\n21\teach set is staged and how a pointer resolves.\n22\t\n23\tEvery following code fence is illustrative. [`tests/guides.test.ts`](../tests/guides.test.ts)\n24\tkeeps the command reference aligned with the executable and transcribes the pure blueprint-default,\n25\tcompile-refusal, and error-narrowing fences. A trailing comment in another fence is this guide's\n26\tclaim rather than a measured answer; the driven examples are the ones the shipped declarations\n27\tprint. Limits states what that leaves unproven and what covers it instead.\n28\t\n29\t```sh\n30\tnpm install --save-dev @orkestrel/scaffold\n31\t```\n32\t\n33\tThe executable needs Node 22.12 or later. Run it through `npx` without installing:\n34\t\n35\t```sh\n36\tnpx @orkestrel/scaffold --help\n37\t```\n38\t\n39\t## Surface\n40\t\n41\t### Core\n42\t\n43\tExported from `@orkestrel/scaffold`, and reachable from\n44\t[`src/core/index.ts`](../src/core/index.ts).\n45\t\n46\t#### Types\n47\t\n48\t| Name                | Kind | Summary                                                                                          |\n49\t| ------------------- | ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n50\t| `Artifact`          | type | One file in a plan, discriminated by how its content is produced and what scaffold claims of it. |\n51\t| `BuildFormat`       | type | One module format a published library environment builds.                                        |\n52\t| `CatalogEntry`      | type | One package row of the fleet catalog.                                                            |\n53\t| `CompileStage`      | type | The compile phases, in the order they run.                                                       |\n54\t| `CompilerEventMap`  | type | The compiler's observation channel.                                                              |\n55\t| `HostFile`          | type | One vendored file read from the repository, beside the target bytes it answers for.              |\n56\t| `Drift`             | type | How one target path compares to the artifact planned for it.                                     |\n57\t| `Environment`       | type | One environment a generated workspace selects on its `src` or `app` axis.                        |\n58\t| `Finding`           | type | One drift verdict against a target path.                                                         |\n59\t| `Group`             | type | The artifact group a plan selects over.                                                          |\n60\t| `Lookup`            | type | How an upstream lookup resolved: found, missing, unmatched, or failed.                           |"}]},"uuid":"08375ac6-dda7-456a-8d5d-9ddf023de0bc","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:34.571Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1c720222-0739-411c-9a95-14a10a737d9e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

Run `scaffold repair`, or `npm update` followed by the same audit, until no question remains.\n1161\t3. Run the gate chain, then bump the version and publish.\n1162\t\n1163\tThat cycle is what keeps every release shipping the then-latest floor, and it is what a consumer\n1164\tgenerating a workspace with no network receives.\n1165\t\n1166\t## Vendored data root\n1167\t\n1168\tThe vendored data root is the shared file set, staged into the published package as plain data.\n1169\tStaging walks `HOST_PATHS` and `CANON_PATHS`, and a release ships what both name.\n1170\t\n1171\t`HOST_PATHS` is the vendored set, and a target receives a copy of each path it selects: the licence,\n1172\tthe harness wiring, the bench scripts, the shared policy register, the byte-identical root dotfiles,\n1173\tand the guide mirrors a generated workspace starts from. It is a candidate list rather than a plan,\n1174\tbecause a workspace never mirrors its own guide.\n1175\t\n1176\t`CANON_PATHS` is the instruction canon, staged for reading instead: the `AGENTS.md` coding contract,\n1177\tthe `CLAUDE.md` harness bridge, the `.agents/orchestration.md` agent-operation contract, the rules\n1178\tunder `.claude/rules/`, the skills under `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/`, the templates under\n1179\t`.agents/templates/`, and the transport contracts under `.agents/transports/`. A release stages every\n1180\tone of them and no plan claims those staged bytes, so no target receives a copy of a contract. At the\n1181\t`AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` destinations a target receives the pointers instead: different content at\n1182\tthe same paths, planned as this package's own template content. A reader reaches the contracts from a\n1183\tscaffold checkout sitting beside the repository, or from the\n1184\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/` root inside the installed package, and the `AGENTS.md`\n1185\tpointer scaffold plans into a target names each location.\n1186\t\n1187\t`HOST_PATHS` and `CANON_PATHS` are disjoint. A path in both would be copied into a target as a host\n1188\tartifact and refused by the overlay that keeps a host artifact current, at once. `isCanonPath` is the\n1189\tone reading of canon membership, matching a member and anything beneath a member that is a directory,\n1190\tso staging, the live overlay, and the executable's advisory never disagree about what a path is. The\n1191\tdocument compiler deliberately plans the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers at canon destinations\n1192\tas this package's own template content, and the advisory subtracts exactly the paths that compiler\n1193\tplans.\n1194\t\n1195\tThe `host.json` file at the repository root is the committed live inventory. Each entry carries the\n1196\tSHA-256 digest of its file content, and the inventory carries a membership digest over its declared\n1197\tpaths and file digests. Run `npm run build:inventory` whenever a vendored byte or path changes; the\n1198\t`config` project refuses a stale inventory. Run that gate against a quiescent checkout: its fresh and\n1199\tcommitted reads cannot distinguish stale data from a source edit made while the gate runs.\n1200\t\n1201\tThe installed release fixes which paths a target owns. A live inventory can update bytes only for\n1202\tthose paths; it can neither introduce a path nor delete one. A path added upstream is invisible\n1203\tuntil a release adds it to the installed manifest. Remove a vendored path in the same change that\n1204\tships the release which removes it from that manifest.\n1205\t\n1206\tMoving a path from `HOST_PATHS` to `CANON_PATHS` is not that removal. The path stays staged, stays in\n1207\tthe installed manifest, and keeps being published; what changes is that no host artifact claims it. A\n1208\ttarget generated before the move keeps the copy it received, and where no artifact is planned at that\n1209\tpath no verb writes or deletes that copy, so `audit` raises the `canon` question naming it until a\n1210\tmaintainer removes it in one commit.\n1211\t\n1212\tAt the default `UpstreamOptions.retries` value, an aligned target spends one request on `host.json`,\n1213\tand each installed path whose live digest differs from the target adds one request for its bytes. A\n1214\tpositive `retries` value can repeat a request after a transport fault. Raw-host propagation lag after\n1215\ta commit is a property of the content host. Scaffold neither creates that lag nor presents a stale\n1216\tresponse as fresher than the host served it.\n1217\t\n1218\tA canon destination costs no request. No target receives those bytes, so the fetch list drops the\n1219\tpath rather than spending a round trip on one no verb can write, and `filesToHost` takes the\n1220\tinstalled floor bytes for it. A fill carrying no row for a canon path is complete rather than\n1221\tspoiled, which is what lets one `Host` carry live bytes beside floor bytes without mixing baselines\n1222\twithin a surface.\n1223\t\n1224\t`.claude/settings.json` is in that set, and the artifact planned for it is content-owned. `repair`\n1225\tand `overwrite` restore its bytes, so an edit made to it inside a target is reverted at the next\n1226\tvisit and reported as drift until then. Put an operator grant in `.claude/settings.local.json`\n1227\tinstead. That path is outside `HOST_PATHS` and matches the vendoring deny-list\n1228\t`matchesSensitivePath` reads, so `stageHost` never copies it into a host root and no plan carries\n1229\tit.\n1230\t\n1231\t`stageHost` fills the root from a real checkout at build time:\n1232\t\n1233\t```ts\n1234\timport { stageHost } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n1235\t\n1236\tstageHost(process.cwd(), 'dist/host') // one ManifestEntry per file staged\n1237\t```\n1238\t\n1239\tEach staged path is copied to a storage name, and every dot that opens a segment comes off,\n1240\tbecause npm's own ignore rules would drop a leading-dot entry from the tarball. A dotted file at the\n1241\troot moves under `dotfiles/` so it cannot collide with an undotted sibling. `manifest.json` is\n1242\twritten last and declares the whole membership: one entry per file with a digest computed from the\n1243\tstaged destination after its copy, the sorted directory inventory, and a SHA-256 digest over both.\n1244\tThe membership digest detects an edit that did not update the manifest, and the directory inventory\n1245\tmakes a declared empty directory survive a file walk.\n1246\t\n1247\tThe pointer reads that spelling back. The installed branch of a target's `AGENTS.md` names the\n1248\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/AGENTS.md` file, the\n1249\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/orchestration.md` file, and the\n1250\t`node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/claude/rules/` directory, which are where the dot-stripped\n1251\tstorage names of `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`, and `.claude/rules/` land. A reader\n1252\tfollowing a canon path through the installed package types the storage spelling, not the repository\n1253\tone.\n1254\t\n1255\tA missing staged path is refused rather than staged around, and the refusal names every missing\n1256\tpath at once. That is why `guides/scaffold.md` — this file — must exist before `npm run build`\n1257\tcompletes.\n1258\t\n1259\tThe `Materializer` reads the root once, at construction, and cross-checks the manifest against the\n1260\tfiles actually stored. It defaults to the root inside the installed package, resolved from the\n1261\tmodule's own location rather than from the caller's working directory. `--from` points it somewhere\n1262\telse. A root carrying no manifest at all is read as a raw checkout, and artifact paths map onto it\n1263\tone to one.\n1264\t\n1265\t### Integrity\n1266\t\n1267\tHTTPS supplies Transport Layer Security (TLS) for each fetched response, and the reader applies its\n1268\tper-response and per-call byte budgets before it accepts content. It carries each fetched vendored\n1269\tresponse's decoded content as hexadecimal before any character decoding and verifies that content\n1270\tagainst the digest in `host.json`, then verifies the inventory against its membership digest.\n1271\tTransport encoding is transparent and does not enter the comparison. The path never character-\n1272\tdecodes and re-encodes the content.\n1273\t\n1274\tThis posture supplies integrity, not authenticity. An attacker who can serve the files can also\n1275\tserve a matching inventory. The residual is direct: fetched bytes govern agent behavior in a target\n1276\tthat has no release gate. Run `audit` to preview the change, use the `--offline` option to pin the\n1277\tdistributed floors, and keep operator grants in `.claude/settings.local.json`; scaffold does not\n1278\tread or write that file.\n1279\t"}]},"uuid":"b37b3fd2-66e8-4f89-b57f-5e9b96b1d1a9","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:38.003Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cc97d173-e9b8-4f62-8602-6be11124efa1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

The verdicts that are measured are the ones a consumer hovers:\n1492\t[`tests/distribution.test.ts`](../tests/distribution.test.ts) drives every `@example` the built\n1493\tdeclarations print against the installed package, scores each verdict it can read as a value, and\n1494\tnames exactly the ones it cannot. Executing the remaining fences would require fixtures for each\n1495\tambient value plus isolated filesystem and network drivers for the mutating examples; adding those\n1496\tdrivers is separate test capability rather than name-resolution parity.\n1497\t\n1498\t**The library does not enforce the creating verb's policy.** `new` refuses a blueprint carrying any\n1499\tquestion, and `materialize` writes any plan into any vacant target. The Compile section states the\n1500\trule a library caller applies in its place.\n1501\t\n1502\t**No verb removes a superseded instruction copy.** A target generated before the canon split holds\n1503\tits own copies of the paths `CANON_PATHS` names. `repair` writes the planned paths a target is\n1504\tmissing or has let drift and never deletes, so it replaces the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` copies\n1505\twith the pointers planned there and leaves every other copy alone. `overwrite` does delete a tracked\n1506\tfile the plan does not own, but its foreign reading extends only beneath the vendored directories the\n1507\tplan expands, and a plan expands no canon directory, so those copies sit outside what it can see.\n1508\tThey are unmanaged: `audit` reports no drift against them and raises the non-blocking `canon`\n1509\tquestion naming them instead. The removal is one commit in the target — `git rm -r` over each named\n1510\tpath — and the release wave's per-repository visit is where it runs.\n1511\t\n1512\t**`isPath` does not prove host portability.** It proves bounded target-relative syntax and rejects\n1513\ttraversal, separators, controls, and reserved syntax characters. It deliberately admits host-specific\n1514\tsegment spellings such as a Windows device name, a trailing dot or space, and a segment beyond a\n1515\tfilesystem's byte ceiling. The compiler emits none of those names. A caller-supplied plan may carry\n1516\tone, and the writer reports the host's refusal rather than treating logical path syntax as a promise\n1517\tthat every filesystem can create it. `isFilesystemPath` is the separate server guard for target and\n1518\tvendored-root locations on the host. Both `/` and `\\` are separators to every reading in this\n1519\tpackage, on every host, including the server's reading of a raw symbolic-link target. A POSIX\n1520\tfilename that legally contains a backslash — `weird\\..\\name` — is therefore refused as three\n1521\tsegments rather than admitted as one name. That is one separator law with a conservative side, not a\n1522\thost-dependent second one.\n1523\t\n1524\t**Scaffold emits no styles axis.** `SRC_MATRIX` is exactly `core`, `browser`, and `server`, and\n1525\t`Blueprint` carries no styles field. A workspace that needs `src/styles/` adds the directory, its\n1526\tconfiguration, and its Vitest project by hand. `.claude/rules/workspace.md` describes styles as an\n1527\tenvironment because the fleet has one; scaffold does not generate it.\n1528\t\n1529\t**No host path is normalized before it is guarded.** `isFilesystemPath` refuses an empty segment, so\n1530\t`packages//router` is off contract. A trailing separator does not produce one: it terminates a\n1531\tdirectory rather than opening a segment, and every Node path API reads `./packages/router/` and\n1532\t`./packages/router` as one location, so both are admitted. Every server entry point and the\n1533\t`--target` option guard the text they were handed and resolve it afterwards, so a directory taken\n1534\tfrom a shell completion arrives carrying the separator the shell appended and names the directory it\n1535\tappears to name.\n1536\t\n1537\t**A generated workspace has empty barrels and no starter entity.** Every emitted `index.ts` exports\n1538\tnothing. This is deliberate: a generated sample entity is repeatedly mistaken for real\n1539\timplementation. What a consumer does first is write the module's `types.ts`, then the\n1540\timplementation that conforms to it, then export both from the barrel — the order `AGENTS.md` fixes.\n1541\t\n1542\t**Scaffold generates the distribution proof and refuses to generate every other one.** The subject\n1543\tis what separates them, and it is the whole rule. Generating a file is not the same as writing one:\n1544\tscaffold also writes `tests/policy.test.ts` and `tests/config.test.ts` into a target, byte for byte\n1545\tfrom the shared file set, and the distribution proof is the one it derives from the workspace it is\n1546\twriting into.\n1547\t\n1548\tA distribution proof's every assertion derives from the artifact the workspace installs: the\n1549\t`exports` map the packed tarball declares, the built declarations beside it, and the module objects"}]},"uuid":"9f994da6-e9d2-49cb-bc43-cf0a836dd258","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:38.133Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d0e5c684-76ce-474c-a46a-b9686e764373","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

A value that is not the exact shape raises `ScaffoldError` coded\n901\t`INVALID`, because it is not a question anyone can answer. Each entry point snapshots the caller's\n902\tvalue first and then guards the snapshot, so a property backed by an accessor is refused rather than\n903\tread. `isPlan` refuses an artifact at `package.json` unless it carries `birth` ownership, because a\n904\tplan claiming `content` or `presence` there contradicts the compiler-produced plan.\n905\t\n906\tOverrides replace a drafted artifact's content whole. The gate checks each override against the\n907\tblueprint's full draft before a group selection narrows the returned plan, so an override outside a\n908\tselected group does not block that compile. An override that matches no artifact in the full draft,\n909\tthat targets a host-origin artifact, or that targets the manifest is a blocking question rather\n910\tthan a silent no-op.\n911\t\n912\t### Groups\n913\t\n914\tA plan selects over the following groups, and a compile that names none covers all of them. Their\n915\torder is the order a plan lists its artifacts in.\n916\t\n917\t| Group           | Holds                                                              |\n918\t| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |\n919\t| `manifest`      | `package.json`                                                     |\n920\t| `configs`       | The root and per-target build configuration, and the root dotfiles |\n921\t| `source`        | The selected environment barrels and entries                       |\n922\t| `tests`         | The shared setup modules, the entry tests, and the policy sweep    |\n923\t| `guides`        | The guide index and the vendored guide mirrors                     |\n924\t| `docs`          | `README.md` beside the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers        |\n925\t| `orchestration` | The vendored harness wiring, the bench scripts, and `.mcp.json`    |\n926\t\n927\tNo group carries the instruction canon. No host-origin artifact claims a `CANON_PATHS` member, so no\n928\tgroup selection reaches a staged contract and no verb copies one into a target. The one deliberate\n929\toverlap is on the plan: the `docs` group carries the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers that name\n930\twhere each contract is read instead, planned at those canon destinations as this package's own\n931\ttemplate content.\n932\t\n933\t## Ownership and drift\n934\t\n935\t`Origin` and `Ownership` describe every planned file, and they answer different questions. `Origin` says how the\n936\tcontent is produced. `Ownership` says what scaffold claims at the path.\n937\t\n938\t| `Origin`   | Content comes from                          |\n939\t| ---------- | ------------------------------------------- |\n940\t| `host`     | Byte-copied from the vendored data root     |\n941\t| `template` | Filled from a frozen template definition    |\n942\t| `computed` | Derived by this package's combination logic |\n943\t\n944\t| `Ownership` | Audit compares | A write does                                      |\n945\t| ----------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |\n946\t| `content`   | The bytes      | Restore a missing file, replace a stale one       |\n947\t| `presence`  | Existence only | Restore an absent file, never touch present bytes |\n948\t| `birth`     | Nothing        | Create the file only during initial materialize   |\n949\t\n950\tPresence ownership has separate mechanisms, and a reader needs to know which applies:\n951\t\n952\t| Mechanism       | Paths                                             | Bytes belong to                              | Cost                                                        |\n953\t| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |\n954\t| Verb-owned      | `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` and dependency guide mirrors | `catalog` or `mirror`                        | The owning verb is the only route for a later update.       |\n955\t| Workspace-owned | `WORKSPACE_OWNED_PATHS`, which holds `.gitignore` | The target workspace                         | Present bytes receive no later canonical ignore update.     |\n956\t| Plan-owned      | `DISTRIBUTION_TEST_PATH`                          | The plan, until the workspace writes its own | A deleted file is restored from the plan on the next write. |\n957\t| Unhydrated      | Every vendored path a core-compiled plan carries  | Scaffold, after a hydrating face reads them  | Hydration restores the byte claim the core plan omits.      |\n958\t\n959\tThe unhydrated row is the one a core-only caller meets most, and reading it as a claim about the\n960\tpath is the mistake it invites. `Compiler` runs in the pure core face, which cannot read the\n961\tvendored data root, so every host artifact it plans carries `presence`: a claim over bytes nobody\n962\thas read is a claim no comparison could check. A `src: ['core']` plan therefore reports `presence`\n963\tfor `.claude/settings.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `tests/policy.test.ts`, and every other vendored\n964\tpath, and a consumer concluding from that reading that scaffold never replaces those bytes is wrong.\n965\t`Materializer` hydrates the plan before it audits or writes: hydration reads the vendored root and\n966\tturns each path scaffold owns the bytes of into a content-owned artifact, leaving `presence` on the\n967\tworkspace-owned paths and the mirror pointers the preceding rows name. What a verb claims at a\n968\tvendored path is the hydrated ownership, and `HostArtifact` carries the same narrowing on the type.\n969\t\n970\tThe `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` pointers sit outside that row. Their bytes come from a frozen\n971\ttemplate rather than from the data root, so the pure core face already claims them content-owned and\n972\thydration leaves them alone. `repair` and `overwrite` restore a missing pointer and replace a drifted\n973\tone, in every face, which is what keeps a target's resolution instructions in agreement with the\n974\trelease it installed.\n975\t\n976\tBirth ownership is what makes a generated workspace the consumer's. `materialize` writes a\n977\tbirth-owned path into a vacant target. A later `repair` or `overwrite` call treats that path as\n978\taligned whether it is present or absent, so it neither restores missing bytes nor replaces present\n979\tbytes.\n980\t\n981\tYou own `tests/setup.ts`, the selected `tests/setupBrowser.ts`, `tests/setupServer.ts`,\n982\t`tests/setupService.ts`, and `tests/setupGlobal.ts` modules, each root `tests/setup*.test.ts` proof,\n983\tthe selected environment entry tests under `tests/src` and `tests/app`, the\n984\t`tests/src/bin/main.test.ts` file, and the `tests/integration.test.ts` seed. Scaffold writes those\n985\tplanned files only during materialize and leaves later edits or deletions alone. You also own the\n986\t`tests/guides.test.ts`, `tests/conformance.test.ts`, and `tests/service/**/*.test.ts` proof files,\n987\teach of which selects its project by being written. Scaffold content-owns `tests/setupPolicy.ts`,\n988\t`tests/policy.test.ts`, and `tests/config.test.ts`; `repair` and `overwrite` restore those files when\n989\ttheir bytes drift or the files are missing.\n990\t\n991\t`tests/distribution.test.ts` is the one proof scaffold generates, and the one test artifact it\n992\tclaims by presence. Generation is the line, not writing: scaffold writes the vendored\n993\t`tests/policy.test.ts` and `tests/config.test.ts` proofs too, and restores them, but those are the\n994\tshared file set's own bytes copied into the target. The distribution proof is derived from the\n995\tworkspace's own shape instead, which is why it is the one proof a generated file can be. A\n996\tpublishing workspace missing that file reports `missing` drift, and `repair` or `overwrite` writes\n997\tthe generated proof there. A workspace that replaced the generated proof with a better one keeps its\n998\town bytes exactly: presence compares existence, so no verb reads what is already at the path, none\n999\treplaces it, and none ever reports it stale. Deleting the file is how you ask for the generated\n1000\tproof back; editing it is how you keep your own. Limits states what makes this proof generable when\n1001\tthe others are not.\n1002\t\n1003\tContent ownership does not preserve an arbitrary custom Vitest project. The optional proof projects\n1004\tare fixed: `guides`, `integration`, `conformance`, and `service` are selected by their defining\n1005\tpaths, and `distribution` by a published `src` environment. A workspace that needs other local\n1006\tconfiguration must keep those edits outside a content-owned file; `repair` restores that file to the\n1007\tcanonical project set.\n1008\t\n1009\tAn audit reports one `Finding` per planned path, followed by any foreign path beneath the groups\n1010\tthe plan covers. Every planned finding carries its artifact's `ownership`. A foreign finding has\n1011\tno ownership because no artifact was planned for its path. `Ownership` says what scaffold claims at\n1012\ta path, not what one run did there. Counting planned findings by `content`, `presence`, and `birth`\n1013\ttherefore says what audit is entitled to compare and stays the same against a vacant target and a\n1014\trepaired one. What one run compared comes from `ownership`, `drift`, and `observed` together. A\n1015\tcontent-owned finding carrying `observed` had its bytes compared. A content-owned `missing` finding\n1016\tand every presence-owned finding were decided by existence alone. A birth-owned finding was not\n1017\texamined. The foreign findings are exactly the ones no ownership accounts for. The audit stores no\n1018\taggregate tally.\n1019\t"}]},"uuid":"fa829241-e743-450a-a175-4fd86ab3db25","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:41.840Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1e8d6268-86fe-465e-a2c9-47299c79faab","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}
