{"parentUuid":"c45fe237-8ff5-4af1-a111-55f0adc29921","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018H8gkERKHxUGLWfnzv1B1N","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The release wave\n2\t\n3\tClose a fleet-wide goal of every package on registry-served versions as a release wave in layer\n4\torder: visit every\n5\trepository once per round with one procedure, publish each layer in one window, and only then\n6\tprepare the next.\n7\t\n8\t## Visit a repository\n9\t\n10\tRun the visit in this order. A step that reads generated or installed state is invalid before the\n11\tstep that writes it.\n12\t\n13\t1. Re-pin the target's `@orkestrel/scaffold` devDependency and install, so the overwrite runs the\n14\t   current vendored host.\n15\t2. Run `scaffold overwrite`.\n16\t3. Where the target still carries an instruction-canon path, delete it. `scaffold audit` names each\n17\t   one in its `canon` question; run `git rm -r` over the named paths in this visit, and prove the\n18\t   removal with the paths' absence and a clean `git status`. No scaffold verb deletes those copies,\n19\t   and this deletion cannot be deferred past the quality gates: `scaffold overwrite` has already\n20\t   replaced the target's `AGENTS.md` with the pointer, which carries no rule map, so\n21\t   `inspectPolicyRuleMap` reports `the rule map names every rule file` for every file a kept\n22\t   `.claude/rules` directory still holds and the policy sweep is red until this deletion runs.\n23\t4. Force-verify every `@orkestrel` range against a registry sweep taken after the previous layer\n24\t   published.\n25\t5. Run the full install.\n26\t6. Run the mutating `format` script to converge generated writes.\n27\t7. Run the quality gates.\n28\t8. Compare the rebuilt `dist/` against the published tarball for material content.\n29\t\n30\tRestore any unpublished tarball the target is holding before the quality gates run, per\n31\t`.agents/orchestration.md` § Fixing a dependency before it publishes. A distribution proof run\n32\tagainst a local tarball proves the local tarball.\n33\t\n34\tRun visits in parallel slices of disjoint repositories, each slice strictly serial inside itself,\n35\treporting per target. Refuse a failed target, name it, repair it, and re-run it alone.\n36\t\n37\t## Rule on the bump\n38\t\n39\tBump on either trigger: the rebuilt dist differs materially from the published tarball, or the\n40\tfinal runtime dependency set differs from the published packument.\n41\t\n42\t- Test the final dependency set against the packument, never \"did my step move a pin\". The\n43\t  `declare` step inside `scaffold overwrite` re-pins before any later check, so the step-local\n44\t  reading reports nothing moved while the manifest surface did.\n45\t- Treat a re-pinned runtime range as published surface. Without the bump a consumer installs\n46\t  duplicate copies of the moved dependency.\n47\t- Compare material content only, as `.agents/orchestration.md` § What a bump obliges defines it,\n48\t  and take the blast radius of a bump from that section rather than deciding it here.\n49\t- Ship a dist built before the version bump wherever the bump edits no emitted byte. Check that\n50\t  per package rather than assuming it: a package that imports its own `package.json` version into\n51\t  published code emits that version, so its pre-bump dist is stale the moment the version moves.\n52\t  Rebuild after the bump there and pack from the rebuilt tree. The `npm publish --ignore-scripts`\n53\t  command skips `prepack`, so that rebuild is the operator's step rather than the publish's.\n54\t\n55\t## Prepare a layer\n56\t\n57\tAn unpublished package's first version is `0.0.1`. Do not bump it before that first publish. The\n58\tregistry has nothing to serve, so there is no version to move away from, and bumping produces a\n59\tpackage whose history starts at a number nothing explains.\n60\t\n61\tPrepare a published package's layer in this order:\n62\t\n63\t1. **Bump from what the registry serves, not from the local manifest.** A repository's `version`\n64\t   field can sit a release behind what was published from another checkout, and bumping that\n65\t   produces a version the registry already holds, which fails on upload after the whole gate chain\n66\t   has run. Read the registry first.\n67\t2. **Re-pin every `@orkestrel` range to what the registry serves, and install.**\n68\t3. **Sweep the self-pins**, per the following section.\n69\t4. **Run each package's own `prepublishOnly` script to green.**\n70\t5. **Commit and push before the window opens.**\n71\t\n72\tPrepare the next layer only after this one is on the registry. A dependent's new pin cannot\n73\tinstall until the version it names exists, so preparation and publication interleave and cannot be\n74\tbatched ahead.\n75\t\n76\tThe window is for uploads. Every gate, build, install, and commit happens outside it, which is\n77\twhat makes `--ignore-scripts` the right flag at publish time: the artifact was already proved, and\n78\tthe flag is what stops the gate chain running a second time inside the five minutes.\n79\t\n80\t## Sweep the self-pins\n81\t\n82\tA package's own version appears in its source and its tests as a literal, and a bump falsifies\n83\tevery one of them. Run this sweep after the re-pin install, not after the manifest edit.\n84\t\n85\t- `grep` the prior version literal across `tests/` and `src/` in the publishing package, and rule\n86\t  on every hit. A canned packument in a fixture and a looked-up version in a CLI suite carry the\n87\t  version with no tripwire comment beside them, so they surface as a red gate after the bump\n88\t  rather than as a planned edit before it.\n89\t- Move a documented tripwire — a golden digest over generated output — in the same change as the\n90\t  version bump. That is what the tripwire is for.\n91\t- Re-take a generated artifact's digest after the install, because the generated bytes can derive\n92\t  from the installed tree rather than from the manifest. An install that picks up a newly\n93\t  published dependency moves such a digest with no manifest edit at all, and reverting the\n94\t  manifest pin does not restore it.\n95\t- Prove the cause of a moved digest before recording it. Patch the generated bytes back to the\n96\t  prior content and reproduce the prior digest: a digest that stays moved after the pin is\n97\t  reverted has a cause the pin does not explain, and a new digest recorded without that control is\n98\t  a value nobody can check.\n99\t\n100\t## Refresh the registry between layers\n101\t\n102\tRefresh the registry evidence between layers and derive each round's pins from it. A pin can only\n103\tname a version the registry already serves, so a dependency shipping in the same window keeps the\n104\tresolvable previous pin and takes its development-only re-pin after the window closes.\n105\t"}]},"uuid":"b3db070a-ec7b-4931-ad51-b45886c3b544","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:50.917Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c45fe237-8ff5-4af1-a111-55f0adc29921","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"87c38ad8-3e24-4cdd-8acf-034074725c8d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ue4B5aYHxiEFSPQPwTurPA","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Plan — scaffold as single host, reconciled from the design round\n2\t\n3\tReconciled by the Orchestrator on 2026-08-27 from the subjective Opus lane\n4\t(`design-subjective-report.md`), the objective Opus lane (`design-objective-opus-report.md`), and\n5\tthe objective Grok lane (`design-objective-grok-report.md`), all run blind on\n6\t`design-brief.md`. Every load-bearing mechanism below was verified by the Orchestrator against the\n7\tsource before adoption.\n8\t\n9\t## Recorded deviations and substitutions\n10\t\n11\t- The Codex bench is dark this session (`codex` CLI absent). Objective design lanes ran on Opus 5\n12\t  (per the engine-assignment table) and on Cursor Grok (the user's directed substitution for Sol,\n13\t  weighted below Opus and verified). Implementation units route to the Opus `implementer`.\n14\t- The `probe` MCP server failed to connect, so the `prove` tool is unavailable. Fallback probes per\n15\t  `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes stand in, each with its control recorded.\n16\t- The plan departs from `PROPOSAL.md` step 1 on membership: `.claude/agents`, `.codex/agents`,\n17\t  `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules`, `.mcp.json`, and\n18\t  `.claude/settings.json` stay vendored. The mechanical reasons: `Materializer.catalog` throws when\n19\t  `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` is unreadable (`src/server/Materializer.ts:951-954`); with no\n20\t  planned directory member, `audit` can never report a foreign finding and `remove` can never act\n21\t  (`src/server/Materializer.ts:618-654`); `.mcp.json` registers the `probe` server the root\n22\t  contract mandates; and the loading-mechanism decomposition shows these files contribute no bytes\n23\t  to the measured context cost, so removing them buys nothing the proposal's goal names. Each Opus\n24\t  lane reached this independently.\n25\t\n26\t## Agreements adopted from every lane\n27\t\n28\t- `HOST_PATHS` semantics stay \"the set written into a target\"; a new staged-only constant carries\n29\t  the instruction canon. Staging walks both; `stageHost` sorts entries by storage\n30\t  (`src/server/helpers.ts:1464-1465`), so `host.json` and `dist/host` stay byte-identical.\n31\t- The pointer pair ships as content-owned `template`-origin `ContentArtifact` beside\n32\t  `README.md` — never as a vendored member: `stageHost:1454-1460` refuses two vendored paths at one\n33\t  storage name.\n34\t- The pointer's `node_modules` fallback names storage paths: `pathToStorage` strips the dot that\n35\t  opens each segment, so the staged copies sit at `dist/host/AGENTS.md`,\n36\t  `dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`, and `dist/host/claude/rules/`. `PROPOSAL.md:58` is wrong on\n37\t  this and the pointer corrects it.\n38\t- No deletion mechanism ships. `repair` writes only `missing` and `stale`; the superseded copies\n39\t  become unmanaged; the fleet visit performs `git rm -r` and the wave reference documents it.\n40\t- `PROPOSAL.md` is retired at acceptance: deleted from the tree, archived here and in git history.\n41\t\n42\t## Rulings on the disagreements\n43\t\n44\t- **Canon membership** (`CANON_PATHS`): `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.agents/orchestration.md`,\n45\t  `.agents/skills`, `.agents/templates`, `.agents/transports`, `.claude/rules`, `.claude/skills`.\n46\t  The whole `.agents/` tree leaves (objective lane over subjective): a target holds no partial\n47\t  `.agents/` tree, and the pointer's resolution paragraph covers every dangling reference a\n48\t  scaffold-supplied file makes. `.claude/rules` is forced out with the pointer conversion: the\n49\t  rule-map inspection reddens a target holding rules under a pointer `AGENTS.md` with no rule map.\n50\t  `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` leave together: the bridge inspection fails a lone side.\n51\t- **Live overlay**: the fetch filter in `CLI.#host` and the floor-byte retention in `filesToHost`\n52\t  land as a pair. The filter alone would make `filesToHost` return `undefined` on every run\n53\t  (`src/server/helpers.ts:1222`) and silently force the floor — the objective lane's finding,\n54\t  verified.\n55\t- **Superseded advisory** (subjective lane, adopted): `audit` raises a target question naming canon\n56\t  members still present in a target, on the `#setupQuestion` precedent (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1359`) —\n57\t  detection without deletion, self-extinguishing after the sweep.\n58\t- **Policy re-scope**: the vendored skill-family case asserts the relationship against a direct\n59\t  read of the family root (binds where the root exists, passes on absence); the path-population\n60\t  witness swaps from `.claude/rules/names.md` to `.claude/settings.json`; and a scaffold-side\n61\t  mirrored server test asserts the staged inventory carries\n62\t  `agents/skills/orkestrel-falsify/SKILL.md` and `claude/rules/names.md`, closing the\n63\t  scaffold-trees-vanish hole without a repository-identity branch in a vendored file.\n64\t- **ROADMAP**: one sequenced row for the fleet adoption visit (subjective lane over objective; the\n65\t  repository keeps a roadmap and the visit outlives this commit).\n66\t\n67\t## Exit criterion\n68\t\n69\tThe campaign closes when each of these ends implemented, retained, or intentionally excluded on\n70\tevidence:\n71\t\n72\t1. Membership is `HOST_PATHS` plus `CANON_PATHS`; staging carries both; `host.json` is\n73\t   byte-identical to a fresh `stageInventory` without regeneration.\n74\t2. The pointer pair compiles at `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, content-owned, written by `new` and\n75\t   repaired as stale, with storage-correct fallbacks and no `@path` import.\n76\t3. The live overlay excludes canon destinations and still overlays planned ones.\n77\t4. `audit` raises the superseded advisory; no verb deletes canon leftovers.\n78\t5. The vendored policy suite passes on a post-migration target shape and still binds in scaffold;\n79\t   the scaffold-side inventory test binds the canon family.\n80\t6. `catalog`, `audit`, `repair`, and `overwrite` still act against a generated target.\n81\t7. Guide, README, ROADMAP, and every executed fence agree with what shipped; `PROPOSAL.md` is\n82\t   retired.\n83\t8. The gate chain is green: `format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, `test`.\n84\t\n85\tThe release and the per-target visit (re-pin, `repair`, sweep, gates) are the follow-up the wave\n86\treference documents; publishing is the user's decision.\n87\t\n88\t## Routing ledger\n89\t\n90\t| Unit | Subject | Role | Engine |\n91\t| ---- | ------- | ---- | ------ |\n92\t| W1 | Source contract: constants, predicate, templates, compiler, staging, overlay, advisory, and their mirrored tests | `implementer` | Opus 5 (recorded substitution for Sol) |\n93\t| W2 | Fixture and proof shadows: `setupServer`, Materializer and distribution tests, vendored policy re-scope, scaffold-side inventory test | `implementer` | Opus 5 (recorded substitution for Sol) |\n94\t| W3 | Documentation: guide, README, ROADMAP, wave reference, `PROPOSAL.md` retirement | `implementer` | Opus 5 |\n95\t| A1 | Adversarial audit of W1-W3 | `reviewer` + objective lane | Opus 5 + Cursor Grok (user-directed substitution) + `checker` on Sonnet |\n96\t| V1 | Authoritative gates | `verifier` | Sonnet |\n97\t\n98\tOrchestrator-owned: serial integration, the build-and-inventory proof between units, reconciliation\n99\tof the audit, and acceptance.\n100\t"}]},"uuid":"12ff44f5-e951-4993-8569-da2f779a3b0d","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:10:50.960Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"87c38ad8-3e24-4cdd-8acf-034074725c8d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"104628ad-e5ed-4cd8-a698-63d98e38b263","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HhHZTD2xTJUSYiEMYoKAoH","type":"tool_result","content":"625\t * @throws `ScaffoldError('INVALID', …)` when `root` is not a host path.\n626\t * @throws `ScaffoldError('TARGET', …)` when `root` is present but is not a\n627\t * physical directory, cannot be read, holds a name this package could not plan,\n628\t * or carries more entries or more nesting than one inventory may report.\n629\t *\n630\t * @remarks\n631\t * A whole-tree answer throws where a single-path answer returns `undefined`, and\n632\t * the reason is that a partial inventory reads exactly like a complete one. A\n633\t * caller comparing a target against a plan would treat a truncated listing as\n634\t * proof that the missing files are not there.\n635\t *\n636\t * Absence is the one exception: nothing to inventory is a complete answer, so it\n637\t * is the empty list. Links are listed as files rather than followed, so no\n638\t * traversal can leave the root and no cycle can form.\n639\t *\n640\t * @example\n641\t * ```ts\n642\t * import { listFiles } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n643\t *\n644\t * listFiles('./dist/host') // ['AGENTS.md', 'CLAUDE.md', 'LICENSE', …]\n645\t * ```\n646\t */\n647\texport function listFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n648\t\tif (!isFilesystemPath(root)) {\n649\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('INVALID', 'Listing root is not a host path', { root })\n650\t\t}\n651\t\tconst status = attempt(() => lstatSync(root))\n652\t\tif (!status.success) {\n653\t\t\tif (matchesMissingPath(status.error)) return []\n654\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('TARGET', `Listing root cannot be inspected at ${root}`, {\n655\t\t\t\troot,\n656\t\t\t\terror: status.error,\n657\t\t\t})\n658\t\t}\n659\t\tif (!status.value.isDirectory() || status.value.isSymbolicLink()) {\n660\t\t\tthrow new ScaffoldError('TARGET', `Listing root is not a physical directory at ${root}`, {\n661\t\t\t\troot,\n662\t\t\t})\n663\t\t}\n664\t\tconst files: string[] = []"}]},"uuid":"e534ac6e-c71a-4b8a-a519-49c47edda60f","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:14:01.920Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"104628ad-e5ed-4cd8-a698-63d98e38b263","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

{"parentUuid":"f77fb045-e07a-4644-a0fe-4519b75f2fd7","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"2f12594a-28d0-46cd-99d2-3f739e83a3a5","agentId":"a4e5b23dfefd2b8a7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012jMGwZCjhkcxtrh5EfHoAt","type":"tool_result","content":"1205\t *\n1206\t * @example\n1207\t * ```ts\n1208\t * import { filesToHost } from '@orkestrel/scaffold/server'\n1209\t *\n1210\t * // A floor declaring the host-owned `scripts/codex.sh` path and the canon\n1211\t * // `AGENTS.md` destination. The script's live bytes are taken; the canon\n1212\t * // destination keeps the floor's.\n1213\t * filesToHost([{ path: 'scripts/codex.sh', lookup: 'found', hex: '23212f62696e2f73680a' }], floor)\n1214\t * // { manifest: { entries: [ … ], roots: [ … ], digest: '…' },\n1215\t * //   bytes: { 'scripts/codex.sh': '23212f62696e2f73680a', 'AGENTS.md': floor.bytes['AGENTS.md'] } }\n1216\t * ```\n1217\t */\n1218\texport function filesToHost(files: readonly HostFile[], floor: Host): Host | undefined {\n1219\t\tconst declared = new Set(floor.manifest.entries.map((entry) => entry.destination))\n1220\t\tconst held = new Map<string, string>()\n1221\t\tfor (const file of files) {\n1222\t\t\tif (file.lookup !== 'found' || !declared.has(file.path)) return undefined\n1223\t\t\tif (!isDeferredPath(file.path) && !isCanonPath(file.path)) held.set(file.path, file.hex)\n1224\t\t}\n1225\t\tconst entries: ManifestEntry[] = []\n1226\t\tconst bytes: Record<string, string> = {}\n1227\t\tfor (const entry of floor.manifest.entries) {\n1228\t\t\tconst hex =\n1229\t\t\t\tisDeferredPath(entry.destination) || isCanonPath(entry.destination)\n1230\t\t\t\t\t? floor.bytes[entry.destination]\n1231\t\t\t\t\t: held.get(entry.destination)\n1232\t\t\tif (hex === undefined) return undefined\n1233\t\t\tentries.push({\n1234\t\t\t\tstorage: entry.storage,\n1235\t\t\t\tdestination: entry.destination,\n1236\t\t\t\texecutable: entry.executable,\n1237\t\t\t\tdigest: hexToDigest(hex),\n1238\t\t\t})\n1239\t\t\tbytes[entry.destination] = hex\n1240\t\t}\n1241\t\treturn {\n1242\t\t\tmanifest: {\n1243\t\t\t\tentries,\n1244\t\t\t\troots: floor.manifest.roots,\n1245\t\t\t\tdigest: computeManifestDigest(entries, floor.manifest.roots),\n1246\t\t\t},\n1247\t\t\tbytes,\n1248\t\t}\n1249\t}\n1250\t\n1251\t/**\n1252\t * Stage the named destinations of a value host into a private root.\n1253\t *\n1254\t * @param host - The host whose bytes are written, keyed by destination.\n1255\t * @param root - The private directory to fill; it must already be a directory\n1256\t * this process may write into.\n1257\t * @param destinations - The destinations to stage, each declared by `host`.\n1258\t * @returns The entry staged for each destination, in the order requested.\n1259\t * @throws `ScaffoldError('INVALID', …)` when `root` is not a host path or a"}]},"uuid":"68717497-b994-48cf-8339-5f5808b2acec","timestamp":"2026-08-27T08:14:09.902Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f77fb045-e07a-4644-a0fe-4519b75f2fd7","userType":"external","entrypoint":"remote","cwd":"/home/user/scaffold","sessionId":"dc70cba3-3e2a-551f-9e05-1da04bbbf122","version":"2.1.247","gitBranch":"claude/scaffold-proposal-impl-nabmm9"}

I held the **subjective lane**.\n\n---\n\n# Design\n\n## 1. The `.claude/agents` split\n\n**Ruling: `.claude/agents` joins `CANON_PATHS` as a directory; `HOST_PATHS` names nothing beneath it; `nameToHostArtifacts` plans `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` explicitly beside its `HOST_PATHS` selection.**\n\nThe list shape is forced, not chosen. `stageHost` walks `[...HOST_PATHS, ...CANON_PATHS]` and refuses two staged paths at one storage name (`src/server/helpers.ts:1417`, `:1470-1476`). A canon `.claude/agents` directory stages `orkestrel.md` through its file walk, so naming that file in `HOST_PATHS` too makes `npm run build` refuse the stage. Therefore the catalog file cannot sit in `HOST_PATHS`, and the overlap must sit on the **plan**, exactly where the pointer pair's overlap already sits.\n\nThe compiler edit is one path:\n\n```ts\nexport function nameToHostArtifacts(name: string): readonly Artifact[] {\n\treturn [...selectHostPaths(HOST_PATHS, name), CATALOG_AGENT_PATH].map(…)\n}\n```\n\nAdd no new constant. A one-member `PLANNED_CANON_PATHS` would be a rename of `CATALOG_AGENT_PATH` with no boundary, invariant, or narrower contract behind it, which the wrapper test in `.claude/rules/architecture.md` refuses. The reason belongs in the function's `@remarks`, where a reader meets it.\n\n**What `isCanonPath('.claude/agents/orkestrel.md') === true` must not break, checked against the code:**\n\n| Reading | Effect | Verdict |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Fetch filter, `src/bin/CLI.ts:640` | `!isDeferredPath && !isCanonPath`; the path was already excluded as deferred | Unchanged. The predicates now overlap on exactly this path and the conjunction is unaffected. |\n| `filesToHost`, `src/server/helpers.ts:1223`, `:1229` | Same disjunction; floor bytes taken either way | Unchanged. |\n| `Materializer.#expand`, `:662-688` | `matched` is the single manifest entry; `isDeferredPath` makes it a presence artifact | Unchanged. Restore-on-absence still reads the staged bytes through `#copy`. |\n| `Materializer.#roots`, `:639-653` | The artifact's source is a file, not a manifest root, so `.claude/agents` never becomes an owned root | Correct and load-bearing: it is why the sibling role files stop being foreign findings, and why the sweep of question 3 has to reach them another way. |\n| `Materializer.#empty`, `:745-760` | No root equals or descends the file source | Unchanged. |\n| `catalog`, `:380-390` | Reads and rewrites the marker region in place | Unchanged. |\n\n**The invariant, restated.** The shipped sentence \"No host-origin artifact claims a canon path\" goes false, because the second overlap *is* host-origin. Do not patch it by origin. Restate it as three facts with different scopes:\n\n- **List disjointness.** No path is named by both lists, directly or beneath a directory member. This is what staging depends on, and it still holds literally.\n- **Plan overlap.** The plan deliberately claims paths inside the canon, and names them: `blueprintToDocumentArtifacts` claims `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` with this package's own template content, and `nameToHostArtifacts` claims `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` with the canon-staged bytes.\n- **The rule the verbs obey.** A target holds a file at a canon path only where the plan claims it.\n\nThat third sentence is the one to write into `CANON_PATHS` remarks and the guide, because it is the contract the sweep of question 3 makes true and the advisory of question 2 measures.\n\n**On the flag the brief invites:** the owner's keep is correct and I surface no defect against it. Teaching `catalog` to skip on absence would turn a fleet-wide catalog refresh into a per-target silent no-op, replacing an actionable `TARGET` refusal with a run that reports success and wrote nothing. The keep costs one named path in one compiler; the alternative costs the verb its failure signal.\n\n## 2. The advisory's subtraction\n\n**Ruling: subtract the whole plan's path set, not the document artifacts alone; name each canon member present; where the plan claims a path beneath a named directory member, the message names that spared path in an `apart from` clause.**\n\n`#canonQuestion` builds `planned` from `blueprintToDocumentArtifacts` alone (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1424`). Under the new membership that names `.claude/agents` wholesale, and the message tells a maintainer to remove the catalog file `catalog` requires and `repair` puts back. Generalize the subtraction to the compiled plan's artifact paths, which is the fact the advisory was always reaching for.\n\nName the **directory**, with the exception clause. Do not enumerate the target's unplanned children: that message grows with the target's file count and buries the instruction, which is the failure the shipped comment already warns against (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1411-1413`). The exception clause is derived from the plan, so its length is bounded by the plan and stays short as canon membership grows.\n\nThe reading also changes shape: **name a canon member only when a file exists at or beneath it**, never on directory presence alone. `isPhysicalDirectory` (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1428`) keeps reporting an emptied directory forever, and the sweep of question 3 leaves emptied directories behind. Without this change the advisory never goes quiet after the very sweep meant to silence it.\n\n## 3. The `overwrite` sweep\n\n**Ruling: make an unplanned canon path a `foreign` finding in `audit`. Add no deletion method, no `Drift` member, and no report field. Then delete `#canonQuestion`.**\n\n`Materializer.remove` already means \"delete the tracked files the plan does not own\" (`src/server/Materializer.ts:423-477`). A superseded canon copy is exactly that. Routing it through `foreign` inherits, unchanged: the candidate re-derivation and `#reconfirmCandidates` staleness refusal, `matchesProtectedPath`, the tracked-set narrowing, the quarantine transaction that restores what it already took, the `--dirty` waiver, the `removed` result field, and the exit rule the guide already states — \"A foreign file counts as drift\" (`guides/scaffold.md:708-710`). A second deletion verb would split one deletion across two transactions and leave a partial failure half-applied.\n\nThe change is the audit's snapshot population. `#derive` (`src/server/Materializer.ts:618-634`) extends the target reading beneath the vendored directory roots the plan expands. Extend it with a second source: each `CANON_PATHS` member that resolves in the target, its files added when it is a directory and itself when it is a file. Gate that source by the plan's selected groups through `inferGroup`, the same rule that classifies a vendored path and a foreign path, so a `--groups tests` audit still reports nothing under `.claude/`. Planned paths already sit in `paths`, and the set is a `Set`, so the catalog file and the pointer pair pair with their artifacts and never reach `foreign`.\n\n**Semantics, stated exactly:**\n\n- Deletes: a git-tracked file at or beneath a `CANON_PATHS` member, in a selected group, that the plan does not claim.\n- Never deletes: any path the plan claims; any untracked path; any path `matchesProtectedPath` answers for.\n- Recursion into a directory member is by file, which is what spares a planned file beneath a canon directory without a special case.\n- Composition with the existing foreign deletion: one candidate list, one transaction, one `removed` array.\n\n**Deletion by path, not by byte identity.** A digest test against the installed canon bytes would spare exactly the stale copies the sweep exists to take, because a target that is a release behind holds bytes that no longer match. Path membership is the only criterion that closes the loop.\n\n**The untracked leftover is left, and that is a feature.** `remove`'s own reason is stated in its remarks: git is the recovery mechanism, so a path git cannot restore is not one this verb takes. Keeping that law intact gives a maintainer a real seam — see question 4 — and carving a canon exception into it would buy nothing, because a canon copy scaffold wrote into a target is tracked.\n\n**Negative controls the unit must carry:**\n\n| Control | Must report |\n| --- | --- |\n| A drifted `AGENTS.md` pointer in a target | Rewritten by `repair`, absent from `removed` |\n| An edited `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` | Bytes unchanged, file present, markers still found by `catalog` |\n| A tracked `.claude/rules/names.md` leftover | Present in `removed`, absent from the tree, `audit` then exits 0 |\n| A git-ignored `.mcp.json` a maintainer wrote | Present after the run, absent from `removed` |\n| An untracked, unignored canon leftover | `overwrite` refuses the dirty tree without `--dirty`; with `--dirty` the file survives |\n| A planted `src/` and `app/` file | Untouched; the existing protection control stands |\n| A canon leftover under `--groups tests` | Not a candidate |\n\n**Delete `#canonQuestion`.** Once the leftovers are findings, a non-blocking advisory naming the same files in a second channel is a duplicate that drifts. `Question.field` is a plain `string` (`src/core/types.ts:517`), so this removes no union member. The behavioural consequence is deliberate and must be named in the guide: a target holding a superseded copy moves from exit 0 with an advisory to exit 1 with a finding, which is what makes the fleet visit's success condition mechanical.\n\n## 4. The `probe` server in targets\n\n**Ruling: after the sweep the `prove` tool is unavailable in a bare-target session by default, and a maintainer restores it with a git-ignored `.mcp.json`. State that limit in the guide and in the rule that owns the instrument.**\n\nThe registration is the only thing a target loses. `@orkestrel/probe` stays in `BASE_DEV_DEPENDENCIES` (`src/core/constants.ts:445`), so the server binary is installed in every target, and `templates.ts:396-406` still emits the `probe` Vitest project the server arms through. Only `.mcp.json` goes.\n\nThe seam, stated as an instruction: add `.mcp.json` to the target's `.gitignore` — which is workspace-owned, so scaffold never replaces it — and write the `probe` registration there. Ignoring rather than merely leaving the file untracked is required, because `overwrite` reads `git status --untracked-files=all` (`src/bin/CLI.ts:1547-1552`) and refuses an unignored untracked file as a dirty tree.\n\n**This strictly widens what a maintainer can keep.** Today `.mcp.json` is vendored and content-owned, so `overwrite` reverts a target's own registration at every visit. After the sweep an ignored one survives every visit for the first time.\n\n**The honest limit:** a scaffold-primary session cannot prove a claim about a target's TypeScript. The registration in scaffold's `.mcp.json` starts the server with a relative path resolved against scaffold's own working directory, so the server arms against scaffold's `probe` project. I have not read `@orkestrel/probe`'s installed declarations for whether `prove` accepts a workspace root per call; that reading settles whether the limit is absolute or configurable, and the unit takes it before writing the sentence.\n\nCarry the consequence into `.claude/rules/quality.md` § Instruments, which owns the `prove` rule. That rule fires on a question that can supply a project, a case, and a control; it does not say what to do when the server is unregistered. One line closes it: register the server as the preceding paragraph describes, or follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes and report the fallback instrument's control and coverage. Give it one home there and restate it nowhere.\n\n## 5. Codex and Cursor sessions on scaffold\n\n**Confirmed for the sweeps. Reported as unknown for the bare-target session.**\n\nNo inspection binds `.codex` or `.cursor` presence in a target. `inspectPolicyWorkspace` (`tests/setupPolicy.ts:1943`) composes the mirror, suppression, source, skill-family, bridge, rule-map, filename, script, line-ending, and portability inspections. The rule-map inspection reads `.claude/rules` and `AGENTS.md`; the skill inspections read `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills`, and the shipped control at `tests/policy.test.ts:522-558` already proves the whole suite silent on a target holding the pointer pair, `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, and no canon tree. `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB` (`tests/setupPolicy.ts:274`) names `.codex` and `.cursor`, but it is a path-name population: it reports on the files it finds and passes on absence. The vendored-file import law is untouched — `configs/policy.ts` imports nothing and `configs/helpers.ts` imports nothing a target lacks. `tests/config.test.ts` names none of these trees.\n\n**One dangling reference survives the sweep and the design must close it.** `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md:12` reads \"Read `.agents/orchestration.md` first\", and that file stays in every target while `.agents/` does not. Rewrite that line to the installed-package spelling the pointer pair uses — `node_modules/@orkestrel/scaffold/dist/host/agents/orchestration.md`, because `pathToStorage` strips the dot that opens each segment. This is the whole goal of the sweep applied to the one harness file a target keeps, and it is not optional.\n\n**Unknown, reported as such.** Whether `codex exec --cd <target>` or a Cursor session opened at a target reads that target's `.codex/config.toml` and `.cursor/rules`, or the orchestrator's, is unmeasurable from this checkout with the Codex bench dark. Treat a bare-target Codex or Cursor session as degraded mode. The settling reading is one bounded `codex exec --cd <target>` carrying a marker only a target-local `developer_instructions` value could produce, taken on the host after the bench comes live.\n\n## 6. Documentation and parity\n\nTwo prose claims go false and must be rewritten to the verb split — `audit` previews, `overwrite` deletes, `repair` never deletes:\n\n- `guides/scaffold.md:689-703`, the whole `canon` question passage. Its subject no longer exists as a question. Exit criterion\n\nThe follow-up closes when each of these ends implemented, retained, or intentionally excluded on evidence:\n\n- `.claude/agents`, `.codex/agents`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.cursor/rules`, and `.mcp.json` sit in `CANON_PATHS`, `HOST_PATHS` names none of them, and the staged inventory is unmoved by the membership move taken alone.\n- The plan claims exactly `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md` inside the canon, and `catalog` still rewrites its table in a generated target.\n- `audit` reports every unplanned canon path present in a target as a group-scoped foreign finding and exits 1 on one; `#canonQuestion` is gone.\n- `overwrite` deletes every tracked one in the run that repairs the pointers, spares every planned path, every untracked path, and `src/` and `app/`, and reports them in `removed`.\n- `repair` deletes nothing.\n- No file scaffold leaves in a target names a path the target does not have.\n- Guide, README, ROADMAP, wave reference, and the `prove` rule agree with what shipped; parity and the executed fences are green.\n- The gate chain is green: `format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, `test`.\n\n---\n\n# Alternatives\n\n**A second `Materializer` deletion method, `sweep`, taking canon leftovers.** Cost: two deletion calls in `#replace`, two transactions, and a partial failure that leaves half the deletion applied with no single rollback; a second candidate-reconfirmation path to keep in agreement with the first; a `removed` array assembled by the caller from two results; and `audit` still unable to preview the canon half without a third reading. The recommended design gets all of that from `remove` unchanged. It wins because the concept — a tracked file the plan does not own — was already named `foreign`, and splitting one concept across two verbs is the drift `AGENTS.md` § Design laws refuses.\n\n**Delete only a canon copy whose bytes match the installed canon.** Cost: it spares exactly the stale copies the sweep exists to take, because a target a release behind holds bytes that no longer match the installed staged copy. It would leave the fleet visit needing the manual `git rm -r` step the follow-up is removing. The recommended path-membership rule wins because it makes a statable invariant true — a target holds a file at a canon path only where the plan claims it — and the maintainer's escape hatch lands on git tracking, where `remove`'s own recovery law already put it.\n\n---\n\n# Units\n\nOne writer at a time in the main checkout, each dispatched from a clean committed baseline.
